- `GhostKpBind` (a KeyPackage extension, signed with the KeyPackage; never in the tree) binds a KeyPackage to one channel, one purpose (1 join, 2 rejoin, 3 restore) and the creator's trusted day; admission refuses a KeyPackage bound to another channel or purpose, or whose day is more than 3 days after the committer's day (σ of Phase 7) or more than 7 days before it (a request's pending window) (§19.3).
- `GhostBootstrap` gives a joiner the current generation. It travels in the Welcome's encrypted GroupInfo through `CommitBuilder::create_group_info_with_extensions` (`om:src/group/mls_group/commit_builder.rs:569`) **if S0 confirms that these extensions reach the Welcome's GroupInfo (V10-8)**; there the committer's GroupInfo signature authenticates it. Otherwise it travels next to the Welcome as `nonce(12) ‖ ChaCha20-Poly1305(K_boot(e), nonce, AD = "ghost/v1/ch/bootstrap" ‖ channel_id ‖ kp_ref, GhostBootstrap)` (§5.6 WELCOME body; corrected §19.16): only members of the join epoch compute `K_boot(e)`, so a holder of `JK_g` — a member removed at the end of g, an invite holder — can neither read nor replace it. Round 10b's fallback, HPKE base mode to the joiner's `box_pk`, authenticated no sender: any holder of `JK_g` could re-seal the same MLS Welcome with its own bootstrap and send the joiner to a namespace it chose. It must never be readable under `JK_g` alone (P-C4(b); mutants PC-11, PC-17). **The joiner checks the bootstrap**: when it names the generation of the namespace the Welcome arrived in (the invite's for a join or restore), `ns(G)` must equal that namespace; a newer generation is accepted only as authenticated by the GroupInfo signature or `K_boot(e)`.

### 5.5 Channel content: the MLS application plaintext `ChannelContent` v1

```
ChannelContent = version(1) = 0x01 || kind(1) || op_id(16) || author_minute(u32_be) || timer_code(1) || tlv*
tlv            = type(u16_be) || len(u16_be) || value
```

- `author_minute`: whole minutes since 1970-01-01T00:00Z on the author's trusted clock (FR-3.4, T13); there is no finer time anywhere in the content.
- Canonical form: TLVs strictly ascending by type, no duplicates, only the TLVs listed for the kind, lengths within bounds, no trailing bytes; otherwise `NON_CANONICAL` and the post is dropped and counted.
- The spec's binding of "channel ID, epoch, content type and client operation ID" (spec §7.3): the channel and epoch are bound by MLS (group id and key schedule), the content type by `kind`, the operation id by `op_id`, all inside the encrypted content. MLS `authenticated_data` is left in clear inside the PrivateMessage by RFC 9420 §6.3 and hidden from relays by the outer seal (§5.6); it is authenticated by MLS (signed in the framed content and bound by the AEAD) and readable by every member of the epoch. **Application messages** carry `ChannelOrder = version(1) = 1 ‖ sender_leaf(u32_be) ‖ seq(u32_be)` there (9 bytes, set through `MlsGroup::set_aad`, which the next `create_message` consumes and resets, `om:src/group/mls_group/mod.rs:326-331`, `application.rs:96,113`): the author's own ordering claim, which a receiver reads before decrypting (`PrivateMessageIn::aad`, `om:src/framing/private_message_in.rs:59`) and which nobody else can alter (§6.3.3, §19.18). **Commits** carry `GhostCommitAad` there (§19.2).

| kind | Name | TLVs | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x01 | POST | 0x0001 `text` (1 … 16 000 bytes UTF-8; with `media_ref`: the caption, optional, ≤ 1 024 bytes of strict UTF-8), 0x0002 `reply_to` (16, optional), 0x0003 `media_ref` (exactly 84 bytes: the fixed part of Phase 11's media descriptor, `faza11-media.md` §4.1 offsets 0 … 83, with `key_slot` zero outside Rust; **refused (`NON_CANONICAL`) until the channel-media slice, §5.10, Q38**) | replies are posts; a POST carries `text`, `media_ref` or both |
| 0x02 | REACTION | 0x0004 `target` (16), 0x0005 `grapheme` (0 … 32 bytes; empty withdraws) | one reaction per (author, target) |
| 0x03 | INVITE_ISSUED | 0x0010 `invite_id` (16), 0x0011 `invite_pk` (32), 0x0012 `expiry_day` (u32, days since 1970), 0x0013 `sponsor_sig` (64: the sponsor's pseudonym signature over `"ghost/v1/ch/invite" ‖ channel_id ‖ invite_id ‖ invite_pk ‖ u32(expiry_day)`, §19.2) | the sponsor's record (§6.2) |
| 0x04 | LEAVE | none | asks a committer to remove the author (§6.6) |
| 0x05 | REVEAL | 0x0020 `hpke_enc` (32), 0x0021 `ciphertext` (≤ 512) | §6.9 |
| 0x06 | FLAG | 0x0030 `target` (32, a pseudonym key), 0x0031 `reason` (u8) | **reserved** (ADR-13, P1); refused in Phase 10 (Q15) |
| 0x07 | PIN | — | reserved (FR-3.7, P2) |

- **Timer.** A receiver deletes a post after `min(its timer_code, the channel timer of the post's epoch)`, so a member cannot lengthen a post's life beyond the policy; the author encrypts with the current policy timer (encrypt-at-session, §6.3.1).
- **Size.** MLS adds about 146 bytes per application message with 0x0003 (measured, `mlsscale` run: 0 → 145, 100 → 246, 3 000 → 3 146 bytes), plus the 9 bytes of `ChannelOrder`. The longest text post (16 000 bytes) fits the 16 KiB bucket (capacity 16 316 bytes, §5.6); posts never need 64 KiB. A media POST (84-byte `media_ref`, caption ≤ 1 024 bytes) is under 1.4 KB and so always takes the class floor, like a short text (Phase 11's R-D1 for DMs, §5.10).

### 5.6 The outer seal (normative; the DM shape of F9 §6.2 with channel labels)

```
blob(B) = salt(16) || hint(16) || ChaCha20-Poly1305(k, nonce = 0^12, AD, P)       |P| = B − 48,  B ∈ {4096, 16384, 65536}
hint    = HMAC-SHA256(R, "ghost/v1/ch-hint" || mode(1) || salt)[0..16]
k       = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = K, salt = salt, info = "ghost/v1/ch-seal" || ns(32), L = 32)
AD      = "ghost/v1/ch-seal" || 0x01 || ns(32) || u32_be(B) || mode(1)                 (not transmitted)
P       = mode(1) || body_len(u24_be) || order(16) || body || zeros                    capacity C(B) = B − 68
order   = epoch(u64_be) || sender_leaf(u32_be) || seq(u32_be)      (mode 0x11; zeros in the other modes; for an application
          message it must equal the epoch of the PrivateMessage header and the ChannelOrder of its MLS AAD, §19.18; for a commit
          sender_leaf and seq are zero)
```

| mode | Key K (recognition R) | Body |
|---|---|---|
| 0x11 EPOCH | `K_ep(e)` (`R_ep(e)`) | an `MlsMessageOut` (PrivateMessage: application message or commit) |
| 0x12 JOIN | `JK_g` (`R_join(g)`) | `JoinRequest` (below) |
| 0x13 WELCOME | `JK_g` (`R_join(g)`) | `WelcomeBlob` (below) |

```
JoinRequest = version(1) = 1 || type(1) ∈ {0x01 join, 0x02 rejoin, 0x03 restore}                  (§19.3)
            || kp_len(u16_be) || key_package (an MlsMessage carrying a KeyPackage, TLS)
            || auth
auth        = invite_id(16) || invite_sig(64)                                          (join, restore)
            | day(u32_be) || last_epoch(u64_be) || last_hash(32) || rejoin_sig(64)       (rejoin)
invite_sig  = Ed25519(invite key, "ghost/v1/ch/join" || channel_id || invite_id || SHA-256(key_package))
rejoin_sig  = Ed25519(pseudonym key, "ghost/v1/ch/rejoin" || channel_id || u32_be(generation) || u32_be(day)
                      || u64_be(last_epoch) || last_hash || SHA-256(key_package))
              // generation: of the namespace the request is sealed into; last_epoch, last_hash: the requester's
              // last final epoch and its winner hash; day: the requester's trusted day
WelcomeBlob = version(1) = 1 || kp_ref_len(u8) || kp_ref (the joiner's KeyPackageRef)
            || welcome_len(u24_be) || welcome (an MlsMessage carrying a Welcome, TLS)
            || boot_flag(1) || [ nonce(12) || ChaCha20-Poly1305(K_boot(e), nonce, AD, GhostBootstrap) (61) ]
              // only if V10-8 fails; K_boot(e) and AD of §5.4 (corrected §19.16; round 10b used HPKE base mode to box_pk)
```

- **CS-S1 (T9).** `|blob| = B` exactly; every length is inside P; the bytes are uniform (salt random, hint a PRF output, key fresh per salt). **Channel blobs and DM blobs have the same shape per blob**; a relay cannot tell them apart by bytes. It can tell **namespaces** apart (declared, §19.22): a channel namespace has as many listeners as fetching members (LC-2), and a large-class namespace holds no blob below 16 KiB (LC-1).
- **CS-S2 (canonical).** A known mode for the key class that matched, lengths within P, all padding zero; otherwise consumed, dropped and counted.
- **CS-S3 (binding).** `k` and `AD` bind the namespace and the bucket: a copy into another namespace or a truncation does not open (the relay store can hold one ciphertext under several namespaces, `relay/crates/storage/src/lib.rs:314-420`).
- **CS-S4 (nonce).** `k` is fresh per salt, so the zero nonce never repeats under one key.
- **CS-S5 (one attempt).** The receiver computes the hints of its candidate (R, mode) pairs and opens with the one that matches; a match whose AEAD fails is a forgery, dropped and counted. Candidates per channel: `R_ep(e)` with mode 0x11 for the current epoch and every retained epoch (≤ 65, §8.3), and `R_join(g)` with modes 0x12 and 0x13 for each listened generation (≤ 3). No trial decryption across keys (the partitioning-oracle argument of F9 R-S5).
- **CS-S6 (recognition vs opening).** Kotlin stores and matches recognition keys only; `K_ep`, `JK_g` and `G_g` are stored wrapped under the channel state key and handed to Rust with the blob.
- **CS-S7 (order is a hint; corrected §19.18).** `order` is authenticated by the seal, but every holder of `K_ep(e)` can seal, so the seal says nothing about who chose it: a member could re-seal another member's MLS message under a false `order`. The authoritative ordering key is therefore the `ChannelOrder` in the message's MLS AAD, which only its author can set. `open` returns both; a blob whose `order` differs from its header epoch or its `ChannelOrder` is dropped and counted before any decryption, and after decryption a `ChannelOrder.sender_leaf` different from the MLS sender (`ProcessedMessage::sender`) drops the message. OpenMLS does not expose the ratchet generation of a received message (`ProcessedMessage` carries group id, epoch, sender, AAD, content and credential, `om:src/framing/validation.rs:500-530`), so `seq` is the author's authenticated claim, not checked against MLS; a lying `seq` misorders only its author's own messages. `order` serves peek ordering only (§6.3.3); it is visible only to holders of `K_ep(e)`, who learn the sender anyway on decryption.
- **Welcomes are "encrypted individually" (spec §7.3):** each joiner gets its own sealed copy (its own salt) of the commit's Welcome, whose group secrets are HPKE-encrypted to that joiner's KeyPackage by MLS.

### 5.7 Size classes and buckets (normative)

`bucket(len) = min{ B ∈ {4096, 16384, 65536} : B ≥ floor(class) ∧ C(B) ≥ len }`; a body larger than C(65536) = 65 468 bytes is refused (`NO_FIT`), which bounds every OpenMLS object GHOST accepts to one blob (and so bounds SRLabs S0-1, §6.2.4).

Measured object sizes, suite 0x0003, OpenMLS 0.9.0, bytes (research probe `mlsscale`, host, 2026-09-25; "sparse" = right after one batch add, "populated" = every parent node set):

| Object | 20 sparse | 20 populated | 100 sparse | 100 populated | Class and bucket |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| KeyPackage | 324 | 324 | 324 | 324 | 4 KiB (join request) |
| Remove 1 member (commit) | 2 076 | 1 010 | 8 706 | 1 244 | small: 4 KiB at ≤ 32 (≈ 3.1 KB interpolated, **S0 measures, V10-4**); large: 16 KiB |
| Self-update (commit) | 2 069 | 1 003 | 8 699 | 1 237 | as remove |
| Add 1 (commit) | 2 397 | 1 331 | 9 027 | 1 565 | as remove |
| Welcome for 1 joiner, tree included | 5 186 | 6 223 | 23 170 | 30 191 | 16 KiB (small), 64 KiB (large) |
| Batch add of N − 1: commit / Welcome | 6 802 / 7 905 | same | 33 034 / 38 049 | same | not used: GHOST caps adds per commit (§6.2.4) |
| Application message overhead | 145–146 | 145–146 | 145–146 | 145–146 | — |

- **Rule (normative; revised §19.18):** a committer builds its commit, and while the commit's body exceeds `C(floor)` it defers, in this order, Add proposals from the end of the pass's list, then the context change, then swaps, then Removes beyond the first (each deferred item waits for the next pass; a removal hold, CO-13, applies only to what the commit carries). A commit with **one** proposal (or none, with a path) that still exceeds the floor is a class violation, which S0 must rule out at the class caps with the **composite worst case** (V10-4: in a maximally blank tree with unmerged leaves, at 32 members in 4 KiB and at 100 in 16 KiB: a forced path plus, one at a time, a Remove, a swap carrying a KeyPackage by value with its `SwapEvidence`, an Add with its `AdmitEvidence`, a context change naming 8 committers and 3 funders; and the reviewer's composite of a context change, a Remove and a swap in one commit, which must split under this rule). The protocol review's estimate for the composite is 3.8–4.3 KB against `C(4096) = 4 028`, so the small class is borderline: if V10-4 shows a single-proposal commit above the floor at 32 members, S0 lowers the small class's cap to the largest measured size that fits and re-asks Q3. A commit received above the floor is invalid (CO-2).
- Consequence (P-C3): in a generation namespace every commit and every short post share the floor bucket, and only Welcomes and long posts use a larger one.

### 5.8 TTL per blob (normative; ADR-27 point 11, ADR-32, deviation X-7)

| Channel timer | off | 5 min | 1 h | 1 d | 7 d (default) | 30 d |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Post TTL (mode 0x11 application) | 2 592 000 | 3 600 | 3 600 | 86 400 | 604 800 | 2 592 000 |
| Handshake TTL (commits, JOIN, WELCOME, `INVITE_ISSUED`, `LEAVE`) | 2 592 000 | **604 800** | **604 800** | **604 800** | 604 800 | 2 592 000 |
| Op deadline | author + 7 d | author + 1 h | author + 1 h | author + 1 d | author + 7 d | author + 7 d |

- Posts follow ADR-27 point 11 exactly. The op deadline is ADR-32 point 3 (`author + min(bucket, 7 d)`); a handshake op's deadline is `author + 7 d`.
- **X-7.** In channels with timers ≤ 1 d, handshake blobs live 7 days so that a member offline longer than the timer can still catch up on membership (otherwise it could never rejoin in-band either); relays can tell those blobs from posts by their TTL there (LC-1). In channels with timers 7 d, 30 d or off, every blob uses one TTL (P-C3). The alternatives (whole channel at 7 d; handshake at the post TTL) are Q12's.
- A timer change moves the post TTL at the change: the first post under the new timer marks it at the relays (as DMs, F9 §11.2 "Expirare / schimbare timer"); between 7 d, 30 d and off this also moves the handshake TTL, so "one TTL per generation namespace" holds per **timer version** (JC-4 keys by it, §19.22). A join request uses the handshake TTL frozen in its invite (§5.9, `handshake_ttl_code`): under an invite issued before a timer change it carries the old TTL, which marks it at the relays as a join under an older invite (declared, LC-1).
- **Administrative posts** `INVITE_ISSUED` and `LEAVE` are application messages that committers need for days (an admission may come up to 7 days after the invite; a leave waits for a pass). They use the **handshake** TTL, not the post TTL (in channels with timer 7 d, 30 d or off the two are equal; in channels with timers ≤ 1 d this extends X-7 to them, so relays can tell them from posts there, L8-C14). Round 10b gave them the post TTL, so in a 5-minute or 1-hour channel an invite or a leave request stayed on relays one hour and a committer that did not list within that hour never admitted the invitee or removed the leaver (§19.21).

### 5.9 Channel invite v1 (`ghost://channel/`; ADR-05's link and QR rules)

```
ChannelInviteV1 = version(1) = 0x01 || suite(u16_be) = 0x0003 || size_class(1) || handshake_ttl_code(1)
                || channel_id(32) || generation(u32_be) || ns(32) || read_base(32) || deposit_base(32) || join_key(32)
                || invite_id(16) || invite_seed(32) || expiry_day(u32_be) || name_len(1) || name(0..48)      // 222..270 bytes
link = "ghost://channel/" || base32(ChannelInviteV1), lowercase, unpadded                                     // ≤ 432 characters
```

- No web host, no identity key in clear, no payment address (T16's rules for invitations applied to channel invites); an invite is single-use (§6.2.4) and expires at `expiry_day ≤ issue day + 7`.
- The invite is a **bearer secret for one generation**: whoever holds it first **joins the channel** (admission is automatic; single use means only that the first presenter wins) and every holder can list and store in `ns_g` and open join traffic until the generation rotates, which §19.7 bounds by rotating after an unconsumed invite expires (L8-C4). QR in person is the recommended path; a link sent inside a GHOST DM is end-to-end encrypted (ADR-05's warning about other messengers applies).
- **The channel name is not carried by default** (Q29, §19.7): `name_len = 0`, so the link is 222 bytes (356 base32 characters after the prefix) and a leaked link does not name the channel; the joiner learns the name from `GhostChannelContext` after admission. If the owner chooses otherwise, the name travels in the link in clear and is declared (L8-C4).

### 5.10 Channel media: the Phase 11 contract (reserved; §19.24, Q38)

Spec FR-3.4 requires posts with "media up to 50 MiB" (P0), and the Phase 11 design (branch `p11-design`, `docs/design/faza11-media.md` at `116cfcd`, §4.3, §5.6, ADR-45) states what it requires of Phase 10. What Phase 10 fixes **now**, so that no format changes later:

1. **The pointer.** POST TLV 0x0003 `media_ref` is exactly the 84 fixed bytes of Phase 11's descriptor (`d_version`, `kind`, `flags`, `class_code`, `object_id`, `key_slot`, `manifest_hash`, faza11 §4.1 offsets 0 … 83); the POST's `text` is the caption (≤ 1 024 bytes of strict UTF-8, Phase 11's R-D3) and is optional when `media_ref` is present (§5.5). The descriptor's parsing rules (R-D3) apply unchanged.
2. **The size.** A media POST fits the class floor (§5.5 "Size"), so P-C3 is unchanged: the pointer post is indistinguishable from a short text. The object's fragments are 64 KiB blobs and never share a namespace with the pointer's generation namespace unless Q38's slice decides so (below).
3. **The key slot** (Phase 11 §4.2 applied to MLS). `MlsNative.encrypt` gains an optional `mediaKeyEnv` and the DM context handle that holds its wrap key: for a POST with `media_ref`, Rust checks that `key_slot` is zero, unwraps `key_env` under `(out, dest_id, object_id, manifest_hash)` with the last two read from the same content, writes `K_f` into the slot, MLS-encrypts and zeroises; `receive` of such a POST wraps `K_f` into a new `key_env` bound to `(in, dest_id, object_id, manifest_hash)`, zeroes the slot and returns the content and `key_env`. The file key never reaches Kotlin. `dest_id` of a channel is `SHA-256("ghost/v1/ch/media-dest" ‖ channel_id)[0..16]` (local only: it appears in no relay-visible value). These two arguments are **not** in P0's closed list (G-M6); they enter it with the slice.
4. **TTL and deadline**: the channel's post TTL for every blob of an object (Phase 11's R-MT1 and R-MT4 with the channel timer version fixed at commit), and the post deadline of §5.8.
5. **What a removed member keeps**: the `key_env` of every object it received before its removal (declared with L8-C18's residue; no object sent after its removal reaches it, P-C4).

**Left to the channel-media slice (Q38), with the constraint each choice meets.** (a) Where fragments live: a per-generation media namespace derived from `G_g` (label `"ghost/v1/ch/media-ns"`, with its own per-slot READ and DEPOSIT secrets, funded like `ns_g` and rotated with it) keeps Phase 11's media lane and fairness rules, which key on `Consumer.MEDIA` namespaces, but adds 3 read pairs per channel (6 while draining), so the 9-channel limit of Q4 would fall to about 5 under `maxReadPairs = 64`; fragments in `ns_g` add no pair but mix 64 KiB fragment bursts and `FETCHED_CAP = 256` pressure with posts. (b) The funders' quota: 256 MiB per registering capability per relay per week (F8:417) is about five 50 MiB videos per channel per week. (c) Relay egress of fan-out (Phase 11's F-M5: a 50 MiB post read by 100 members is about 5 GB). (d) The T25 channel-media cases and FR-3.4's "boundary-size, invalid-media" acceptance tests.

---

## 6. Protocol and state machines (normative)

### 6.1 Channel creation (transaction `CH-CREATE`)

1. The creator's client draws `channel_id`, obtains `ps` from `:identity`, and calls `MlsNative.groupCreate` with the policy (`committers = [P]`, `funders = [P]`, timer 7 d unless chosen, class small, `members_may_invite = 1`, `idle_evict_days = 63`, name).
2. The call returns the change-set, `K_ep(0)`, `G_0` and the derived generation values; in one transaction Kotlin writes `ch_channel`, `ch_generation(0)`, `ch_epoch(0)`, the `mls_kv` rows, registers `ns_0` (`Namespaces.register(ns_0, CHANNEL, set(ns_0, w), listen = true, LOCAL)`), installs READ and DEPOSIT credentials for every relay slot of the set, marks `ns_0` funded (`Funding.set`, §7.5) and requests READ and DEPOSIT registrations for `{c, c + 1}` of the capability the planner will redeem (the F9 §4.8 `REGROWS` rule).
3. No traffic follows from the creation except the funder's redemptions and registrations (Q31's rule of F9: pending registrations count as write work, so the planner redeems as soon as an eligible token exists).

### 6.2 Invitation and admission

#### 6.2.1 Sponsor (`CH-INVITE`, a user action)

A member may invite if `members_may_invite = 1` or it is a committer. The client draws `invite_id` and `invite_seed`, builds the invite (§5.9), signs `sponsor_sig` with its pseudonym key and, in the same transaction, encrypts and enqueues an `INVITE_ISSUED(invite_id, invite_pk, expiry_day, sponsor_sig)` post; the invite link is returned to the UI. The invite link stays in `ch_invite_own` (sponsor side) only until `expiry_day`, for the UI; every member, the sponsor included, records the `INVITE_ISSUED` in `ch_invite_issued`.

#### 6.2.2 Invitee (`CH-ACCEPT`, a user action)

1. Parse strictly; refuse an unknown suite (only 0x0003 in P0), an expired invite, a `channel_id` the client is already a member of (unless in `RESYNC_NEEDED`, `REINVITE_NEEDED` or `REPLACED`, where the invite serves a restore, §6.7, §6.12). The client keeps no list of channels it left (T33), so it cannot tell a **clone** — a new group that reuses a known `channel_id`, created for example by a member removed from the original — from a re-invitation: in either it appears with the pseudonym ADR-04 derives from that id (D12). The accept screen says so (Phase 13 copy: "you appear under the same pseudonym as in any earlier channel with this identifier"); declared, L8-C22 (§19.16).
2. Derive `ps` for `channel_id`; `MlsNative.keyPackage` creates a KeyPackage (day-aligned lifetime `[day − 4, day + 80]`, `GhostLeaf`, `GhostKpBind(channel_id, purpose, day)`, the required capabilities) and returns its global change-set (the init and encryption private keys, wrapped). The purpose is **restore** (3) when the client is in `RESYNC_NEEDED` or `REINVITE_NEEDED` for this channel or has no state for a channel whose pseudonym it may still hold (a restored device, §6.12), otherwise **join** (1).
3. In one transaction: `ch_channel(state = joining)`, `ch_join(invite_id, generation, expiry_day, kp_ref)`, register `ns_g` (listen, LOCAL) with READ and DEPOSIT credentials, and enqueue the `JoinRequest` sealed in mode 0x12 with the invite's handshake TTL and the floor of its class.
4. The invitee lists `ns_g` like any member; it opens only mode 0x12 and 0x13 blobs (it holds `R_join(g)` and no epoch key). At `expiry_day + 1` without a Welcome it deletes the join state and the KeyPackage and stops listening (`JOIN_EXPIRED`).

#### 6.2.3 Committer (`CH-PASS`, §6.4)

At a pass, for each pending join request of the current or a draining generation, in arrival order: check (§6.2.4); if valid, propose the Add by value (a restore or rejoin: a swap of the pseudonym's current leaf) and add its evidence to the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (§19.2); build the commit; seal one WELCOME blob per joiner in the namespace the request came from and **keep them wrapped in the committer's own candidate row**; enqueue only the commit in the pass transaction. **The WELCOME blobs are enqueued in `CH-SETTLE`, after `merge_pending_commit` of the committer's own winning commit**, and the invites are marked consumed there (§19.2; RFC 9750 §5.2.3). A committer whose commit loses deletes the Welcomes and keeps the requests pending for its next pass (their KeyPackages are intact: no joiner processed a Welcome).

#### 6.2.4 Admission checks (normative; bound SRLabs S0-1)

The checks split in two (§19.2). **(A) Evidence, checked by every member as part of CO-2** — a function of the commit, the pre-commit group state and the commit's `GhostCommitAad` only: for every Add, one evidence entry names its `kp_ref`; for a join, `sponsor` is the signature key of a current leaf that this commit does not remove and may invite (`members_may_invite` or a committer), `sponsor_sig` verifies under it, `invite_sig` verifies under `invite_pk` over the KeyPackage, no two Adds of the commit share an `invite_id`, and the pseudonym is not a current member; for a swap (Remove of leaf L and Add of the same pseudonym), a restore carries join evidence as above and a rejoin carries `(day, last_epoch, last_hash, rejoin_sig)` with `rejoin_sig` valid under L's signature key; the KeyPackage has `GhostKpBind` for this channel with the purpose matching the evidence kind. **(B) Admission policy, applied by the admitting committer only** (other members do not repeat it; it depends on the committer's history and clock): the committer **holds the `INVITE_ISSUED`** (it was a member when the invite was issued, so it has processed every later Add of its branch and knows every consumption); the invite is unexpired on the committer's trusted day and not consumed on its branch (`ch_invite_issued.consumed`, set at settle from the AAD of every merged Add); the sponsor is still a member (a merged commit that removes P voids P's unconsumed invites, §19.7); the KeyPackage parses, has suite 0x0003, a `BasicCredential` whose identity equals its 32-byte Ed25519 signature key, a lifetime whose bounds are whole UTC days and span ≤ 84 days and that is valid on the committer's trusted day, `GhostKpBind.day` within `[day − 7, day + 3]`, capabilities that include 0xF0A1, 0xF0A2 and 0xF0A4, **exactly one** leaf extension (`GhostLeaf` with a valid `box_pk` and `refresh_epoch = 0`), total size ≤ 1 024 bytes, and a `kp_ref` and init key never seen before in this channel (`ch_kp_seen`, kept until the KeyPackage's `not_after`); the member count after the commit stays within the class cap. A join-type request whose pseudonym **is** a current member is not refused: with purpose restore it becomes an invite-authorized swap (§6.12). A rejoin request is admitted only under the freshness rules of §6.7. A second join request for the same invite is ignored. A committer admits at most the number of Adds that keeps the commit within the floor (§5.7). Because every object is bounded by one blob and KeyPackage size and extensions are checked, the "50 MiB extension" of S0-1 cannot enter a GHOST channel through an Add; a malicious **member** can still send large valid objects up to 64 KiB per blob (declared, L8-C11).

#### 6.2.5 Joiner (`CH-JOIN`, on a WELCOME blob)

The invitee (or rejoiner) opens a WELCOME blob with `JK_g`, matches `kp_ref`, runs `MlsNative.welcomeJoin` (OpenMLS `JoinBuilder` with `skip_lifetime_validation()`, `om:src/group/mls_group/creation.rs:1313`, then GHOST's structural lifetime check of every leaf, §19.4), verifies `GhostChannelContext` and the bootstrap (authenticated and namespace-checked, §5.4, §19.16), derives `K_ep(e)` and the generation values, and in one transaction writes the group rows, `ch_channel(state = active, joined_epoch = e)`, the generation rows, and deletes the join state. If the bootstrap names a newer generation than the invite's, the namespace change is **not** made here: `CH-SWITCH` makes it at the joiner's next session start (§19.8; `CH-JOIN` calls no `Namespaces`, `Outbox`, `Registrations`, `Funding` or `Capabilities` method, G-M5). No store results (T40): the joiner's first store is its user's first post or its scheduled self-update; its new listening is LC-5. **Rejoin and restore Welcomes** (the client still holds rows of the channel's old group): Kotlin passes `welcomeJoin` only the global rows of the KeyPackage, none of the old group-scoped rows, so OpenMLS's `GroupAlreadyExists` check (`MlsGroup::load` finds nothing, `creation.rs:305-312`) does not fire and `JoinBuilder::replace_old_group()` (`creation.rs:1304`) is not needed; the same transaction deletes the old `mls_kv` rows and writes the change-set (§19.16).

### 6.3 Posting and receiving

#### 6.3.1 Compose and encrypt-at-session (`CH-POST`)

A post is encrypted **only when the channel is current**: the client has, in the present session, completed one listing pass over every relay of the current generation's set and processed every openable blob of its current epoch; it holds **no valid candidate for its current epoch that removes a pseudonym** (Remove, leave, eviction or swap: the **removal hold**, CO-13, §19.6; RFC 9750 §5.2.2 option 1 pauses sending for every candidate, GHOST pauses only where a departing member could read); and it is not `clock_blocked`, `resync_needed` or switching generation (§19.8). **A committer's own pending commit holds its posts only when that commit removes a pseudonym** (revised §19.13; round 10b held every own pending commit, which cost owners and moderators at least 20 minutes of posting after every pass and bought nothing: a post in epoch e while a non-removing commit is pending is a late post of e for members that settled, decrypted from the retained epoch). OpenMLS's `create_message` checks only `is_active()` and the proposal store (`om:src/group/mls_group/application.rs:89-94`) and does **not** call `is_operational` (`mod.rs:1115-1121`), so it would encrypt in epoch e even while a removing commit is pending (E-5 corrected, §19.6); `MlsNative.encrypt` therefore refuses with `PENDING_COMMIT` when the own pending commit removes a pseudonym, as a native backstop of CO-13. Otherwise the post waits as plaintext in `ch_pending_post` (under the content key, the F9 §13.8 pattern) and is encrypted and enqueued in the first transaction where the channel is current. Consequences: posts are almost always encrypted in the epoch their receivers hold, so late posts come only from store delays, not from offline composition; the store minute of a post composed offline depends on the session's catch-up time (declared in LC-7). A pending post that reaches its op deadline without being encrypted fails (`FAILED`, the user may retry).

The post's op: namespace `ns_g` of the current generation, bucket by §5.7, TTL by §5.8, deadline `author + min(bucket, 7 d)`, relays `set(ns_g, week(now))`, the Phase 7 send delay (`SendDelay.OFF` refused for `Consumer.CHANNEL`, as for DMs).

#### 6.3.2 Receive (`CH-RECV`)

The messaging actor drains `Consumer.CHANNEL` like `Consumer.DM` (the listener and the consumer tail are the existing single slots, wired to the one actor, D16). Per channel it `peek`s the fetched rows of its namespaces and classifies each blob by the hint that matches (CS-S5):

| Opened as | Action |
|---|---|
| no match | if some candidate could still appear (a future epoch of this channel, or a Welcome for a pending join): `Inbox.defer(60 s … 6 h, backing off)`, re-offered at once by `Inbox.reofferDeferred` after every settle and join (§7.5, §19.1); counted as *unopenable foreign* for CO-11 only in the **current** generation's namespace and unless it is the client's own (§19.15); at the blob's relay expiry: consume and drop (`unopenable`, counted) |
| 0x11, epoch = current | application message: `order` checked against the header and `ChannelOrder` (CS-S7), decrypt in peek order (§6.3.3), apply content, mark consumed; commit: validation run (§6.4 CO-2), record as candidate with its store-hour anchor (CO-4), mark consumed; **proposal** (header content type): dropped before `process_message` and counted — GHOST never calls `store_pending_proposal` (`om:src/group/mls_group/processing.rs:398`), because one stored proposal would make OpenMLS refuse every post until the next commit (`application.rs:92-94`) (§19.16); a result OpenMLS reports as `OwnPrivateMessage` is never evidence of anything — its sender claim is protected only by the epoch's sender-data key, which every member holds (`om:src/framing/validation.rs:158-172`, `processing.rs:251-277`) — so a blob so reported whose hash is not one of the client's own ops is a forgery, dropped and counted (§19.13) |
| 0x11, epoch < current, retained | application message: decrypt with the past epoch (late post); commit (PrivateMessage header content type): **late candidate** — record a fork signal (hash, day), consume; never processed (OpenMLS rejects non-current-epoch handshake messages before decryption, `om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:53-75`, valn1307) (CO-6) |
| 0x11, epoch older than retention | impossible (no key); falls under "no match" |
| 0x12 | committers: record a pending join, restore or rejoin request; others: consume, ignore (they need nothing from it: the admitting commit carries its evidence, §19.2) |
| 0x13 | joiners: §6.2.5; others: consume, ignore |

#### 6.3.3 Peek ordering (the F9 §7.7 idea for MLS)

Within an epoch, OpenMLS decrypts a sender's application messages out of order within the sender ratchet's tolerance (16). Blobs are fetched in blob-hash order (Phase 7), so a burst of more than 16 posts from one sender could be decrypted out of range. GHOST therefore decrypts the openable application messages of an epoch sorted by `(epoch, sender_leaf, seq)` — the `ChannelOrder` its author put in the MLS AAD, which the seal's `order` must repeat (CS-S7, §19.18) — and holds a gap (a missing `seq` of a sender) for at most 12 minutes before decrypting past it (F9's measured hold). **Corrected (§19.18):** round 10b took the key from the seal alone, which any holder of `K_ep(e)` can re-seal with a false `order` — moving a victim's late post to the front would push its earlier posts past the tolerance, a loss of another member's posts. With the key in the MLS AAD, a re-sealed copy with a false `order` is dropped at `open`, and a lying `seq` only misorders the liar's own messages. A relay that withholds part of a burst past the hold can still push the withheld posts out of range (declared, §6.3.6).

#### 6.3.4 Dedup and replay (normative; the four layers)

1. Phase 7 `(namespace, hash)` tombstones (IN-2).
2. MLS: a replayed PrivateMessage fails decryption once its generation key was used (RFC 9420 §9.2 deletion schedule); a resurrected commit of a finalized epoch has the hash already merged and is ignored; a commit of an older epoch without a retained key does not open.
3. The seal binds namespace and bucket (CS-S3): a copy into another namespace, including after a namespace is removed and re-added or a relay re-added (F7:47-52), does not open.
4. `ch_seen(op_id)`: an `op_id` already displayed is dropped (a re-encryption after FAILED keeps its `op_id`), retained as `dm_seen` (F9 §7.6) with the channel's bucket.

#### 6.3.5 Bounds (CS-C8)

Past epochs ≤ 64 and ≤ the retention of §8.3; commit candidates ≤ 8 per epoch, at most one per sender (the lowest hash of that sender), the best 8 by (rank, hash), plus the own commit; fork signals ≤ 16 per channel (the oldest dropped), deleted with their epoch's key; pending join, restore and rejoin requests ≤ 64 per channel, each until its invite's expiry or 7 days after its `day`; `ch_kp_seen` ≤ 1 024 per channel, each until its KeyPackage's `not_after`; pending posts ≤ 256 per channel; own box keys ≤ 66 per channel (the current leaf's and one per retained epoch, §19.17); peek limit 128 per pass; trial settles ≤ 8 per settle and trial decryptions ≤ 32 per candidate (CO-3); `ch_seen` rows bounded by retention.

#### 6.3.6 Declared losses (CS-C5)

A post is lost for a member when: its blob expired at the relays before the member listed it (offline beyond the TTL); its epoch's keys were deleted before it arrived (a store delayed beyond §8.3's retention); its sender was on another branch (§6.7); it is a pending post that reached its deadline; it was stored in a draining generation older than the newest draining one after a second rotation (§6.5); the member rejoined (posts of the epochs it missed on the dead branch or while `RESYNC_NEEDED` are not recovered); or **it fell out of the sender ratchet's range** (§19.18): after the 12-minute gap hold the member decrypts past a missing `seq`, and a post that arrives more than 16 generations behind its sender's newest decrypted post no longer decrypts — a relay can cause this by withholding part of a burst of more than 16 posts from one sender until the hold ends.

### 6.4 Commit arbitration (normative; ADR-42)

*(Rewritten by the review rounds: CO-2 … CO-7 and CO-10 revised, CO-11 … CO-14 added in round 10b; CO-1 … CO-4, CO-8, CO-10 … CO-12 revised in round 10c; the defects and the reasons are in §19.1, §19.2, §19.5, §19.6, §19.13 … §19.15.)*

- **CO-1 (who may commit).** A commit is a candidate only if its sender is a member. Rank = the sender's index in `committers` (0 … 7) or 255 otherwise, **always read from the group context before the commit** (a commit that changes `committers` is judged, and ranked against its competitors, by the ranks it found; §19.13). A rank-255 commit is valid only if it covers **no proposal** (an empty commit with a path: a self-update) and satisfies CO-14. External commits (sender type `new_member_commit`) are dropped before processing.
- **CO-2 (validity: a function of the commit, the pre-commit group state and the commit's `GhostCommitAad` only; checked on a validation run whose change-set is discarded).** OpenMLS accepts it at the current epoch — if OpenMLS refuses it **only** for a KeyPackage lifetime (`LifetimeError`, the device-clock site of §3.6), the candidate is *lifetime-pending*: ranked as valid, never merged, and the member is `clock_blocked` while it would win (§19.4); its body fits `C(floor)`; its authenticated data is a canonical `GhostCommitAad` (§19.2) whose evidence satisfies §6.2.4 (A) for every Add and swap; every commit with a path carries an update-path leaf whose `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch` equals the commit's epoch (§19.5); **every leaf the commit installs keeps its member's identity**: the update-path leaf has the committer's pre-commit signature key and a `BasicCredential` whose identity equals that key (RFC 9420 §5.3.1's successor rule, which OpenMLS leaves to the application, `public_group/validation.rs:890-895`), and a swap's new leaf has the removed leaf's signature key (§19.14); a Remove by rank r targets only non-committers or committers of rank > r, **except a swap** — a Remove of leaf L and an Add of L's own pseudonym with rejoin or restore evidence, which any committer may commit for a member of any rank (the pseudonym keeps its place in `committers` and `funders`, its key being unchanged; §19.13); a GroupContextExtensions change comes from rank 0, except that any committer may drop from `committers` and `funders` the keys its commit removes, and any committer may drop **its own** key from them (a resignation), provided `committers` stays non-empty (§19.13); rank 0 may reorder `committers` (a transfer of rank 0); the rotation flag is set only by a committer (§6.5); the resulting `GhostChannelContext` is valid (§5.3). **Nothing in CO-2 reads the member's history, its `INVITE_ISSUED` records or its trusted clock**: expiry, single use and freshness are the admitting committer's policy (§6.2.4 (B), §6.7), so members admitted at different times judge a commit alike (RFC 9750 §5.3: honest clients "arrive at the same 'first valid Commit'").
- **CO-3 (winner).** Among the valid candidates for epoch e held at settle, excluding **abandoned** ones (a candidate whose own committer has an authenticated message that opens under another candidate's trial state: that committer moved on), the winner **maximises the number of successor senders**, then minimises `(rank, SHA-256(MLS commit bytes))`. The successor senders of candidate X are the distinct authenticated MLS senders — other than X's committer and the member itself — of the held blobs (up to 32, not the member's own) that open under `R_ep(e + 1)` of a **trial settle** of X (`MlsNative.settle` in trial mode: the change-set is not written; Kotlin composes the stored rows and the trial change-set in memory and runs `receive` over them). The trial runs only when more than one valid candidate is held and some held blob does not open under the member's current keys; otherwise every count is 0 and CO-3 is the `(rank, hash)` rule. **A committer's own candidate without a verified copy** (CO-4) ranks below every held foreign candidate unless it has more successor senders than that candidate: other members may never have seen it (§19.13). A candidate that lost is kept only until the settle transaction ends.
- **CO-4 (settle point).** A member settles epoch e at the first actor step where (i) `now ≥ anchor(e) + 20 min`, with `anchor(e)` the earliest, over the held candidates, of min(the member's first sighting, the copy's **relay store hour** + 60 min), where the store hour is `relayExpiryHour − handshake TTL` of the copy it fetched, when the inbox row carries an expiry (otherwise only the sighting counts) (the relay rounds the expiry up to the hour, F7:485, so the store happened no later); and (ii) after (i) held, the member completed one listing pass over every relay of the current generation's set, so it holds every candidate stored before that pass. For its **own** commit the committer also requires a verified copy (the op's outcome is SENT or DEGRADED); if another candidate wins, it clears its pending commit (`clear_pending_commit`) and merges the winner; if its op ends FAILED (no copy can exist, F7's contract), it clears the pending commit. **Revised (§19.13): an own commit that may have reached a relay is never cleared on its own.** While the op is INDETERMINATE, and after it closes INDETERMINATE (Phase 7 resends the identical bytes at most twice), the committer keeps the commit pending, keeps listening, and at each settle point (a) merges it if it wins with a verified copy or with at least one successor sender in its trial settle (CO-3; for an unverified own commit the trial runs whenever a held blob does not open, even if it is the only candidate) — other members merged it and continued, which proves a copy existed; (b) clears it when another candidate wins at a settle point; (c) clears it when no copy can remain on any relay: the op's deadline plus the handshake TTL has passed with neither. Until then its own passes wait (CO-7) — the next rank picks up the pending work under CO-8's work-age rule — and its posts wait only if the commit removes someone (CO-13); the wait is rare, because Phase 7 closes a store INDETERMINATE only when no receipt arrived after its resends. Round 10b cleared it when the op closed without a verified copy; a copy others had merged then stranded the committer, because OpenMLS cannot open the author's own PrivateMessages (`OwnPrivateMessage`, `om:src/framing/validation.rs:158-172`) and so could never adopt its own winning commit later. The committer recognises its own blobs by their hashes (`ch_candidate.hash`, `ch_outbound_op`), never through `process_message`. A lifetime-pending winner blocks the settle until the system clock accepts it. **Catch-up:** a returning member's anchors lie in the past, so each epoch settles right after the session's listing pass and the re-offer of deferred blobs (§7.5): N missed epochs cost N settles in one session, not N × 20 minutes.
- **CO-5 (merge).** The settle transaction (`CH-SETTLE`, time-triggered, local) re-processes the winner with persistence and merges it (`merge_staged_commit` or `merge_pending_commit`), derives `K_ep(e + 1)`, records `ch_epoch(e + 1, winner hash, epoch_authenticator)` and the epoch log, applies the rotation rule of §6.5 (the namespace switch itself is `CH-SWITCH`, §19.8), marks consumed invites and sponsors from the winner's `GhostCommitAad`, re-offers the channel's deferred blobs (`Inbox.reofferDeferred`), and deletes the other candidates. If the winner is the member's **own** commit, it enqueues that commit's WELCOME blobs in the same transaction (§6.2.3, §19.2). Epoch e is then **final** at this member.
- **CO-6 (late candidates; finality is sticky).** A member never leaves a final epoch because of a later commit. A commit-typed blob that opens under the seal of a final epoch e with a hash different from its winner is a **late candidate**: recorded as `ch_fork_signal(e, hash, first_seen_day)` and consumed. It is never validated, ranked or merged: OpenMLS rejects a handshake message whose epoch is not the current one before decrypting it (`validate_framing`, `om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:53-75`, valn1307), so its sender and validity cannot be known without a copy of the previous group state, which GHOST does not keep (§0.4). Anyone who held `K_ep(e)` — a member removed at e + 1 included — can forge one; fork signals therefore feed only diagnostics (`status()`), never a state change.
- **CO-7 (pass preconditions).** A committer runs a pass only when the channel is current (§6.3.1), it holds no pending commit, and it holds **no candidate of rank ≤ its own** for its current epoch (a rank-255 candidate does not block a pass: the committer's commit outranks it, §19.5).
- **CO-8 (pass schedule; D9; revised §19.13).** Every committer runs a pass at its own membership or policy actions (immediately, a user action, whatever its rank: a moderator's Remove does not wait for the owner), at each foreground session after catch-up plus U[0, 5 min] at most once per 6 h, and in a background job at most once per 24 h. Rank 0's scheduled pass commits **if work is pending** (join, restore and rejoin requests, leave requests, evictions due, a queued removal or policy change, a class change, a due rotation of §6.5), and otherwise only if its last commit is ≥ 7 days old (the PCS commit, empty with a path; **≥ 1 day in channels with timers ≤ 1 d**, Q36, §19.19). A committer of rank r ≥ 1 includes in a scheduled pass only **work it first recorded at least `r × 48 h` earlier** (so a moderator handles what the owner left pending for 48 h, the next moderator what both left for 96 h), plus its own PCS commit when its last commit is ≥ 7 days old (the daily cadence of short-timer channels is rank 0's alone: one committer's commit ends the epoch for everyone). Round 10b let rank r pass only while no lower rank had committed in `r × 48 h`; since rank 0 commits only weekly in a quiet channel, rank 1 was "active" about five days in seven and raced the owner on fresh requests routinely, not rarely. The work-age rule needs no liveness signal and races only when the owner returns to work it left for 48 h.
- **CO-9 (self-updates).** A member of rank 255 commits an empty self-update once every 28 days, at the first session after its PRF slot (`HMAC(K_sched, channel_id ‖ u32(period))`, `K_sched` from the MESSAGING branch), and again at the next session after 24 h if it lost the tie-break; it skips a slot at which CO-14 would refuse it.
- **CO-10 (bounded concurrency).** Concurrent commits arise between committers acting on the same aged work (a lower rank returning to work a higher rank picked up under CO-8's work-age rule), between a committer's user action and another's pass or PCS commit, and between self-updates; candidates that meet before the settle point are resolved by CO-3 (the lower rank wins; the loser's work stays pending for its next pass); a late candidate never moves a settled member (CO-6); branches that diverged anyway are handled by CO-11 and CO-12.
- **CO-11 (branch death → `resync_needed`).** A member is on a **dead branch** when, over at least 72 h of first sightings covering at least 3 of its sessions: (a) at least 3 blobs that are not its own, from at least 2 operators, in the namespace of the channel's **current** generation stayed unopenable under every key it holds (revised §19.15: blobs in a draining namespace do not count, so members removed at the end of the old generation, who can still deposit there, cannot push anyone into `resync_needed`; invite holders of the current generation still can, L8-C11); and (b) there was **no progress**: no commit from another member was merged, and authenticated messages opened in its current epoch or later came from fewer than min(2, members of its branch − 1) distinct other members. It then enters `resync_needed` (shown; the rejoin of §6.7 at the user's next action). Progress afterwards (a merged commit by another member, or the messages of (b)) returns it to `active` and withdraws its pending rejoin: a member pushed there by unopenable spam loses only a request that committers refuse (its claimed epoch is their current one, §6.7).
- **CO-12 (split, partition and stale signals).** `split_suspected`: CO-11 (a) holds while progress continues — both branches are alive; the member stays on its branch. `partition_suspected`: no commit by any committer merged for 9 days (the weekly PCS commit plus two days) while authenticated messages from at least 2 other members arrived — the committers may be cut off by the relays. `stale_suspected` (added §19.15): no commit by any committer merged **and** no authenticated message from any other member opened for 9 days, while `committers` names someone other than the member — the silent case the other rules miss: a member that missed a rotating commit which has since expired keeps listening to the old namespace, which drains and goes quiet, so nothing unopenable ever arrives (it also fires, harmlessly, in a channel whose committers and members are all idle). All three are flags shown by the UI with the **fingerprint** `(epoch, first 8 bytes of epoch_authenticator)` of the latest final epoch, for comparison out of band (RFC 9750 §5.2: a partition by the delivery service "can be detected only by out-of-band comparison"), and with the user's `resync` action. None changes posting or causes traffic by itself. A `resync` after `stale_suspected` reaches committers only while they still list the member's namespace (the drain, 14 days after the switch); later the member is `reinvite_needed` (§6.7).
- **CO-13 (removal hold).** While a member holds a valid candidate for its current epoch that removes a pseudonym (Remove, leave, eviction, swap), the channel is not current for posting (§6.3.1); posts wait as plaintext and are encrypted in e + 1 after the settle. The hold lasts until the settle point (≤ 20 minutes plus one listing pass, or at once when catching up) (§19.6).
- **CO-14 (rank-255 rate).** A rank-255 commit for epoch e is valid only if its sender's leaf before the commit has `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch ≤ e − k`, with `k = min(8, n)` and n the member count before the commit; a KeyPackage leaf (`refresh_epoch = 0`) satisfies it once `e ≥ k`. The rule reads only the tree, so every member computes it alike (§19.5).

**Why a settle window and sticky finality, not revert.** Reverting a merged epoch would re-decide posts already displayed and posts already encrypted under it; the settle window costs ≤ 20 minutes per membership change and nothing when catching up. RFC 9750 §5.2.2 describes this option ("pause … to see if any other Commits are received for the same epoch … a deterministic tie-breaking policy"). Its other option — merge at once and keep "a copy of the previous group state" — is refused: the copies "must be deleted within a reasonable amount of time to ensure that the protocol provides forward secrecy", and with a copy and the recorded commits a seized device re-derives every later epoch, read posts included. Without such a copy a late commit cannot be judged at all, so a tie-break applied after the fact (round 10a's "fork evidence, better") could only be forged or ground (§19.1): finality is therefore sticky, and a branch is abandoned only on evidence that it is dead. Relays that serve different commits to different members (possible under AD-1; a delivery service can always partition MLS) produce branches that CO-11 and CO-12 detect when their traffic meets and that the committer-liveness rule bounds when it does not.

### 6.5 Generations and rotation (normative; ADR-41)

- **Rotation rule (revised §19.7, §19.16).** A merged commit opens generation g + 1 iff it removes a pseudonym that it does not re-add (Remove, leave, eviction), carries a **restore** swap (its `SwapEvidence` kind 2: the replaced leaf belonged to a lost, possibly stolen device, which holds `G_g`, the READ and DEPOSIT secrets and `JK_g` — LIMITE:39 treats it as revoked), changes `size_class`, or carries the **rotation flag**; a **rejoin** swap (kind 1, fork repair: the old leaf's state is on the same device) does not rotate. The flag is a bit of the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (committers only, CO-2). A committer sets the flag at a pass when an invite issued in g has expired unconsumed (its holders no longer need the generation's secrets) or g is 28 days old, and no generation of the channel is draining (Q28). Every member computes the rule from the commit alone; joiners learn `(g, G_g, E_g)` from the bootstrap.
- **At the merge (`CH-SETTLE`):** derive `G_{g+1}` from the new epoch and store `ch_generation(g + 1, pending_switch, switch_hour)`, with `switch_hour = ceil_hour(commit_minute + 30 min)` from the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (clamped to at most the settle + 2 h). **The switch is `CH-SWITCH` (§19.8), never the settle**: at the first listing step at or after `switch_hour`, or at the next session start if the member settled after `switch_hour`, it registers `ns_{g+1}` with its relay set, installs its READ and DEPOSIT credentials for every slot, sets `ch_generation(g + 1, current)` and `ch_generation(g, draining, drain_until_day = day(switch_hour) + 14)` — the day of the commit's switch hour, so a member that switches late (or a removed member, which computes it from the commit that removed it) ends the drain on the same day as everyone. A member's first listing of `ns_{g+1}` therefore follows the commit's hour, not the minute it fetched the commit. Posts composed between the settle and the switch wait as plaintext (§6.3.1); ops already enqueued for `ns_g` complete there.
- **At most two generations listened per channel.** A switch while a generation is already draining ends the older drain at once (its unfetched posts are a declared loss, §6.3.6), so a channel costs at most 6 read pairs (§7.7).
- **Funding at a rotation:** the member whose commit rotated funds `ns_{g+1}` for the current week at its switch (it acted; the time is its own commit's hour), and the listed funders from their next EXPIRING renewal only — a non-rotating funder creates registration rows for `ns_{g+1}` in its renewal step, never at the settle or the switch (§19.8; otherwise F9 Q31's "redeem as soon as eligible" would make it a reflex, mutant PC-6). Until a registration lands, stores to `ns_{g+1}` wait (`wait_capability`) and complete when it does; if the rotating committer has no eligible token, the new generation waits up to a week for a funder's renewal (declared, L8-C6). Who funds a new generation is Q31.
- **Drain.** Members list `ns_g` until `drain_until_day` (14 days: the 7-day store window plus 2σ of Phase 7's 3-day clock tolerance, rounded up), then `Namespaces.remove(ns_g)` and delete `G_g`, `JK_g` and the generation's credentials. `Namespaces.remove` returns false while the namespace has operations or fetched, unconsumed rows (`ghost/android/sync/.../api/Namespaces.kt:55-61`), so `CH-SWEEP` first marks consumed every fetched row of `ns_g` still deferred (by then unopenable: its epoch keys are past retention) and retries the removal at the next sweep while an op for `ns_g` is still open (§19.23). Funders stop renewing `ns_g`; its registrations lapse at `start(w + 2) + 1 h` (F9 §4.4).
- **What a removed member keeps.** The old generation's namespace and secrets: it **keeps listing `ns_g` on its schedule until `drain_until_day`** like every member (fetching and dropping; §19.8 — stopping at its own merge would be a fetch-time reflex that names it), and sees the volume of stale posts (of epochs it belonged to; the removal hold keeps posts after the removing commit out of its epochs, CO-13) and of join traffic addressed to `ns_g`. It holds nothing of `ns_{g+1}` (P-C4(b)). That it never lists `ns_{g+1}` still singles it out under AD-1 (LC-8).

### 6.6 Leave, remove, idle eviction

- **Leave** (`CH-LEAVE`, user): enqueue a `LEAVE` post (handshake TTL, §5.8), set `state = leaving`, stop posting; when the op resolves (any outcome), delete the MLS state, keep listening until the generation's drain ends (as a removed member, §6.5), keep local posts until their timers. A committer removes the leaver at its next pass (a leave request is pending work).
- **A committer leaves in two steps (§19.13).** A commit cannot remove its own committer (RFC 9420 §12.2; OpenMLS `CannotRemoveSelf`, `om:src/group/mls_group/commit_builder.rs:808-810`) and CO-2 lets a rank remove only higher ranks, so round 10b's owner could never leave. Now: a committer of rank r ≥ 1 first commits its **resignation** (a context change dropping its own key from `committers` and `funders`, CO-2), then leaves as a member; rank 0 first **transfers** rank 0 (a context change reordering `committers` so that another committer is at rank 0 and itself at a higher rank or out), then leaves the same way. The UI refuses a leave by the only committer of a channel with other members until it names a successor (Phase 13); the only member left in a channel leaves by deleting its state (no traffic). Both steps are user actions, not reflexes.
- **Remove** (committer, user action): an immediate pass with the Remove proposals (and the `committers`/`funders` update when needed); the removed member, on merging the commit, sees itself removed (OpenMLS marks the group inactive), sets `state = removed` — or `replaced` when the same commit adds its own pseudonym with a KeyPackage it did not make (§6.7) — deletes the MLS state and keeps listening, fetching and dropping, until the generation's drain ends (§6.5).
- **Idle eviction.** A committer keeps, per member, the day of the member's last leaf refresh (join, rejoin, own self-update or own commit), `ch_member_refresh(channel, pseudonym, day)`; a member idle longer than `idle_evict_days` (default 63 = 2 × 28 + 7, at most 70, §19.4) is removed at the next pass (RFC 9420 §16.6 "Applications SHOULD have mechanisms for evicting group members that are offline for too long"; RFC 9750 §8.2.2). The evicted member may return through a new invite. A committer is evicted only by a lower rank (CO-2), so **an idle owner is never evicted**: if its device state leaked, its stale leaf keeps receiving every later path secret. Whether an absent owner can be succeeded is Q37 (§19.13; default no, declared L8-C16). The protocol review suggested replacing `ch_member_refresh` by the tree's `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch`; it is **not** adopted: a KeyPackage leaf carries `refresh_epoch = 0`, so the tree does not show when a member that never updated joined, and a committer would still need its own epoch-to-day log (§19.13).

### 6.7 Resync and fork recovery (FR-3.2; TM S3)

| Situation | Detection | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| Restart | none needed | state is persisted atomically (CS-C1) |
| Missed commits still on relays | blobs that do not open are deferred; the next epoch's commit arrives | in epoch order, within the session: posts, then the winner settled by CO-4 right after the listing pass (the anchors lie in the past), then `Inbox.reofferDeferred` opens the next epoch's blobs (§19.1) |
| Missed commits past their TTL | CO-11: foreign blobs stay unopenable, no progress | `RESYNC_NEEDED` (shown to the user) |
| Fork, this member's branch dead | CO-11 | `RESYNC_NEEDED` |
| Fork, both branches alive | CO-12 `split_suspected` | the member stays; the UI shows the fingerprint for out-of-band comparison and offers `resync` |
| Relay partition that keeps the branches apart (e.g. a removing commit withheld from a subset) | CO-12 `partition_suspected` after 9 days without a committer commit; on the other side, `split_suspected` once the subset's posts outlive the retention of their epoch (≤ 2 d + 72 h) while the old namespace drains | out of band; `resync` (declared, L8-C17) |
| Silent orphan: the member missed a rotating commit that has since expired, and its old namespace drained and went quiet (nothing unopenable arrives, nothing opens) | CO-12 `stale_suspected` after 9 days without a committer commit or any other member's message (§19.15) | out of band; `resync` while the committers still drain its namespace, otherwise `REINVITE_NEEDED` |
| Rejoin not answered within 14 days, or the generation drained | no Welcome | `REINVITE_NEEDED`: any member's new invite admits the pseudonym as a **restore** swap (§6.2.4, §6.12) |
| Own leaf swapped by a KeyPackage this client did not make | a merged commit removes its leaf and adds its pseudonym with a `kp_ref` not in `ch_join` | `REPLACED` (shown: "another device with your pseudonym took your place"); state deleted, posts kept; return by a new invite (restore) (§19.3) |

**Rejoin (`CH-REJOIN`, a user action or the user's next action in the channel; never a reflex; revised §19.3).** Create a fresh KeyPackage for the same pseudonym (purpose rejoin, `GhostKpBind`), sign `rejoin_sig` over (channel_id, the target generation, its trusted day, its last final epoch and that epoch's winner hash, SHA-256 of the KeyPackage) and enqueue a `JoinRequest(type = rejoin)` sealed in mode 0x12, one copy in each generation namespace the member listens to (current and draining; a fork at a rotating commit leaves the two sides with different new generations, and only the older one is shared); keep the old state and the KeyPackage's private keys until a Welcome arrives or 7 days pass, then send a fresh request; `REINVITE_NEEDED` after 14 days. **A committer admits a rejoin** (policy) only if: the pseudonym is a current member of its branch; `rejoin_sig` verifies under that leaf's signature key; `day ∈ [its day − 7, its day + 3]`; the `kp_ref` and the init key were never seen in this channel (`ch_kp_seen`); `GhostKpBind` names this channel and purpose rejoin; the claimed `(last_epoch, last_hash)` is not a point the requester can still catch up from — `last_epoch` < its current epoch and either its epoch log holds a different winner for `last_epoch` (a fork) or the commit that closed `last_epoch` was finalized more than one handshake TTL ago or is older than the log (the requester cannot catch up in-band); and no authenticated message from that pseudonym's current leaf opened on its branch in an epoch after `last_epoch` (a live leaf is never replaced by a rejoin). It swaps with the rejoin evidence in the commit's `GhostCommitAad`, where CO-2 verifies the signature (the old leaf removed and the new KeyPackage added in one commit; no rotation). **Any committer may swap a member of any rank** (§19.13): a committer or the owner on a dead branch rejoins like any member and keeps its rank, since its key is unchanged; round 10b's CO-2 let only a lower rank remove a committer's leaf, so rank 0 could never be readmitted after losing a fork. These rules are GHOST's: OpenMLS's `swap_members` "does not enforce that the removed `members` and new members … correspond" (`om:src/group/mls_group/membership.rs:61-71`), and its key-uniqueness check leaves out the removed leaves (`om:src/group/public_group/validation.rs:206-225`). On the Welcome, the member replaces its MLS state (the old group-scoped rows are not passed to `welcomeJoin` and are deleted in the same transaction, §6.2.5), keeps its displayed posts, adopts the bootstrap's generation (switching at its next session start, §19.8) and resumes. OpenMLS's `fork-resolution` helpers are not needed.

**What a replay can still do.** A copied rejoin request re-presents the requester's own KeyPackage, whose private keys the requester keeps for the 7-day window: if admitted, it completes the requester's own rejoin. A join-purpose KeyPackage cannot be re-wrapped as a rejoin (no pseudonym signature) or as a restore (`GhostKpBind.purpose`, checked by CO-2). A holder of the pseudonym seed (a thief, L8-C9) can still rejoin as the member once the member's leaf is quiet; the member then sees `REPLACED`.

**What stays open (declared).** A member on a dead branch loses its posts of that branch for the others; a committer on a dead branch (a backup that committed while partitioned) rejoins like any member; members removed at the end of generation g, who hold `JK_g`, see that a rejoin happened in `ns_g` and the rejoiner's KeyPackage (its pseudonym) during the drain (L8-C5); two colluding members can, in a channel quiet since an epoch's winner, attract a returning member to a fabricated branch that they keep alive (CO-3's successor count excludes a candidate's own committer, not a second colluder), which that member sees as `split_suspected` once the real branch's traffic reaches it (L8-C21).

### 6.8 PCS and self-updates

Committers update their leaf with every commit: an Add-only commit needs no path in RFC 9420 §12.4 (OpenMLS includes one only when a proposal requires it, `om:src/group/mls_group/membership.rs` doc of `add_members`), so GHOST builds every commit with `CommitBuilder::force_self_update(true)` (`commit_builder.rs:287`); S0 measures the size cost (V10-4). Committers therefore refresh at least every 7 days (every day in channels with timers ≤ 1 d, Q36, §19.19), members every 28 days (CO-9). A committer's PCS commit does **not** shut out a seized member device: that device's leaf is still in the tree and receives the new path secrets until the member is removed or replaced; what the cadence bounds is how far back a seized device's current epoch reaches (§19.19). **PCS is scoped (§19.3):** a member whose device state was exposed and then recovered heals at its next self-update or rejoin **only against an attacker who did not obtain the pseudonym seed**; the pseudonym signing key is derived from the root seed and never rotates, so whoever read the seed keeps it, signs rejoins and swaps itself in as the member (L8-C9) — only a new identity heals that. A removed member's state heals nothing for it (it is out). The 28-day cadence is Q9.

### 6.9 Reveal (`CH-REVEAL`, a user action; ADR-04, LIMITE:135)

1. The user chooses a member M. The client builds `RevealPayload = version(1) = 1 ‖ channel_id(32) ‖ P_author(32) ‖ P_M(32) ‖ minute(u32) ‖ card_v2(131) ‖ sig_id(64) ‖ sig_ps(64)` (360 bytes), where `sig_id` is the `ghost1` identity key's Ed25519 signature over `"ghost/v1/ch/reveal-id" ‖ channel_id ‖ P_author ‖ P_M ‖ minute` (made in Rust with the DM context's identity key, the ADR-35 X-21 rule), and `sig_ps` the pseudonym key's signature over `"ghost/v1/ch/reveal-ps" ‖ channel_id ‖ SHA-256(card_v2) ‖ P_M ‖ minute`.
2. HPKE (RFC 9180) base mode, KEM 0x0020 (DHKEM X25519, HKDF-SHA256), KDF 0x0001, AEAD 0x0003 (ChaCha20-Poly1305), through the provider's `hpke_seal` (the HPKE code MLS already uses), `info = "ghost/v1/ch/reveal" ‖ channel_id`, `aad = op_id`, to `box_pk(M)` as M's current leaf publishes it; the result goes in a `REVEAL` post.
3. Every member tries its box key of the post's epoch on each `REVEAL` (one HPKE open per reveal post; `ch_box_key` keeps each box key until the retention of the last epoch whose tree held that leaf ends, §19.17); M verifies both signatures, `P_M` = its own key and `P_author` = the post's MLS sender, and offers "add contact" through `Messaging.addContact(card, CardSource.LINK, …)` — trust `unverified`, the existing source for a card received through another channel (`ghost/android/messaging/.../api/Messaging.kt:350-361`; corrected §19.17: round 10b's `CardSource.CHANNEL_REVEAL` does not exist, `dm_contact.source` has a frozen CHECK, `Schema.kt:928`, and a new value would leave a lasting "came from a channel" mark on the contact).
4. **What members learn:** the author revealed to someone. **What M learns and keeps forever:** the author's DM card and a **transferable** proof that the pseudonym belongs to that identity: `sig_id` and `sig_ps` verify for anyone M shows them to (an operator, a court, other members), and the REVEAL post's MLS signature already proves which pseudonym sent it (L8-C8, the L7-D25 residue in a new form; the deniable alternative is Q35). Nothing crosses the channel otherwise (TB-7).
5. **Forward secrecy of reveals (§19.17).** `box_pk` is fresh in every leaf the member creates (its KeyPackage and each of its update paths) and its private key is deleted when the last epoch holding that leaf leaves retention. A later compromise of M's seed or device therefore opens no REVEAL addressed to an earlier leaf; round 10b derived `b_sk` from the pseudonym seed, so a member who archived REVEAL posts could open every past reveal to M after stealing M's seed. **Retention:** `ch_reveal` rows (sender and recipient side) follow the REVEAL post's timer and carry no time column; the card itself lives on in the DM contact list only if M adds it.

### 6.10 Governance primitives delivered in Phase 10 (D17)

| Primitive | Phase 10 | Later |
|---|---|---|
| Roles (owner, moderators = committers), policy object | yes (§5.3, CO-1, CO-2) | UX in Phase 13 |
| Remove, leave, idle eviction | yes (§6.6) | — |
| Sponsor attribution | yes: `ch_sponsor(member, sponsor, invite_id, day)` from the admitting commit's `GhostCommitAad` (sponsor-signed, so every member, joiners included, records the same attribution; §19.2) | strikes (P1) |
| Threshold function and the S5 simulation | yes: `threshold(n_active, policy)` as a pure function (ADR-13: `max(N, fraction)`, age weighting, sponsor-subtree diversity) and a simulation of Sybil brigades over it (TM S5) | wiring to real flags (Phase 13) |
| Flags, quarantine, strikes, exclusion lists, verified channels | reserved formats only (kind 0x06) | Phase 13 with ADR-13's UX (Q15) |

### 6.11 State machines (each has a JVM test of every legal and illegal transition)

| Machine | States | Driven by |
|---|---|---|
| Channel (member view) | joining → active → {leaving → left, removed, replaced, resync_needed → active (rejoin, or progress resumed, CO-11) or reinvite_needed → active}; joining → join_expired. Flags on `active`: `split_suspected`, `partition_suspected`, `stale_suspected`, `clock_blocked`, `removal_hold` (CO-12, CO-13, §19.4, §19.15) | CH-ACCEPT, CH-JOIN, CH-SETTLE, CH-RECV, CH-LEAVE, CH-REJOIN, SWEEP |
| Role (own) | member ↔ committer (rank r); committer → resigned (a member, CO-2); rank 0 → rank r ≥ 1 by its own transfer; a committer's `leave` is refused until it resigned or transferred (§6.6, §19.13) | CH-PASS (policy), CH-SETTLE |
| Epoch | open → candidates → final (sticky; later commits for it are fork signals, CO-6) | CH-RECV, CH-PASS, CH-SETTLE |
| Own commit | pending → merged (won with a verified copy, or successors seen; Welcomes enqueued) / cleared (lost at a settle point, FAILED, or no copy can remain after deadline + handshake TTL; Welcomes deleted); never cleared merely because the op closed INDETERMINATE (CO-4, §19.13) | CH-PASS, CH-SETTLE, OUTCOME |
| Generation | pending_switch → current → draining → removed | CH-SETTLE (rotation), CH-SWITCH, SWEEP |
| Post (outgoing) | pending_plaintext → queued → sent / degraded / failed / indeterminate; failed → queued (user retry) | CH-POST, OUTCOME, SWEEP |
| Post (incoming) | unread → read → deleted | CH-RECV, DISPLAY, SWEEP |
| Join request (committer) | pending → proposed (own commit pending) → admitted (own commit won; invite consumed, Welcome enqueued) or back to pending (lost) / ignored (expired, consumed, invalid, sponsor removed) | CH-RECV, CH-PASS, CH-SETTLE |
| Invite (sponsor) | issued → expired | CH-INVITE, SWEEP |
| Registration | the F9 §13.5 machine, for funded namespaces | work lane |

### 6.12 Device revocation, restore, wipe (C-6; LIMITE:39)

- **Restore from seed.** Channel state is not backed up (T4), so a restored client has no channel. A member's new invite readmits it as a **restore** (§19.3): its KeyPackage has purpose restore, its pseudonym (derived from the `channel_id` in the invite) is a current member, so the committer swaps its leaf with the invite's sponsor-signed evidence in the commit's `GhostCommitAad` (no freshness rule applies: the invite authorizes it; any committer may swap any rank, the owner included, §19.13); the lost device's leaf is gone and **the lost device decrypts no later epoch** (the LIMITE:39 test, in SCN-M20). **The restore rotates the generation** (§6.5, corrected §19.16): round 10b did not, so the lost — possibly stolen — stack kept the current generation's `G_g`, READ and DEPOSIT secrets and `JK_g` for the rest of the generation (listing volume and timing, filling the quota, opening every later joiner's KeyPackage under `JK_g`), and the T38 oracle's "no later generation" clause passed vacuously for the device-swap case. If the lost device is still running, it sees `REPLACED` and, like a removed member, keeps listening to the old namespace until its drain ends.
- **Stolen device.** The thief holds the seed-derived pseudonym key and can rejoin as the member; a committer cannot tell the thief from the owner. Real exclusion is: the owner asks a committer out of band to remove the pseudonym (a rotation) and returns with a new identity (ADR-14's "identitate nouă" after compromise). Declared, L8-C9.
- **Wipe.** Deletes every channel row, `mls_kv` and `mls_global_kv`; no traffic; the pseudonym is evicted after 63 days.

---

## 7. Relay, sync and entitlement integration

### 7.1 Namespaces (normative)

| Namespace | Registered by | `Namespaces.register` | Read | Write |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| current `ns_g` | every member at accept (`CH-ACCEPT`) and at the switch (`CH-SWITCH`, §19.8) | `(ns_g, CHANNEL, set(ns_g, w), listen = true, LOCAL)`; the rotating committer `Funding.set(ns_g, true)` at its switch, other funders in their renewal step | READ credential of the relay's slot | DEPOSIT credential of the relay's slot |
| channel media namespace | reserved for the channel-media slice (§5.10, Q38); not registered in P0 | — | — | — |
| draining `ns_{g−1}` | every member, removed and leaving members included | listening until `drain_until_day`, then `remove`; at most one draining generation per channel (§6.5) | READ | DEPOSIT, only for ops already enqueued |
| invite `ns_g` (invitee) | the invitee at accept | `(ns_g, CHANNEL, set(ns_g, w), listen = true, LOCAL)` | READ | DEPOSIT (its join request) |

### 7.2 Relay sets

`set(ns, w)` is F9 §5.2's public keyed permutation of the week's ES slots (≥ 2 operators, 3 relays, the 6-week union after a slot-table change), used unchanged for channel namespaces (its HMAC label names DM, but the function takes any namespace; the same vectors apply). **Slot moves (declared, L8-C23; §19.22):** a credential names the ES slot, not the relay (F9's L7-D26); after a slot moves to another operator, the operator that left can present the channel's READ and DEPOSIT secrets it was shown at the slot's new relay until the generation rotates — at most 28 days by the rotation flag (Q28), sooner at any removal.

### 7.3 Credentials: Phase 9's relay features, unchanged

READ (`list`, `get`, `check`) and DEPOSIT (`store`, `check`) bearer credentials v3, their verifiers, `RegisterKey` with the `{cap_week, cap_week + 1}` rule, accumulation, `keys.redb` and the deposit ledger (F9 §4.2–§4.6) serve channels without change. `get` with a READ credential returns `uploaded_at` floored to the ISO week and the exact expiry (`relay/crates/node/src/lib.rs:463-469`), which the unread deadline needs. **No new RPC, kind, field or observable** (§7.8).

### 7.4 Funding and token accounting (normative)

- **Who funds.** The policy's `funders` (default: the owner; up to 3). A funder, for every relay r of `set(ns_g, w)`, keeps READ and DEPOSIT registration rows for `week(now)` and `{c, c + 1}` of its usable **registration capability** of week c (F9 §4.8's `REGROWS` rule, generalised to funded namespaces). Registration capabilities are redeemed by the Phase 8 planner at the EXPIRING renewal time (a PRF time in the last 23 h of the week) and, when a registration is pending without one, as soon as an eligible token exists (F9 Q31). They are stored apart from store credentials (`relay_register_capability`, §9) and used **only** by the registration step (mutant PC-10).
- **Tokens.** One ACCESS token per (relay of the set, funded namespace, week) per funder: **3 tokens per week per channel per funder**, plus 3 for the rotating committer at each rotation it performs — removals, restores (§19.16), and the periodic and invite-expiry rotations of §6.5, at most one per 14 days when no removal happens (Q28). A pack gives 16 per slot per week and the client's own DM namespaces take ≤ 2 (F9 §4.8; 3 once Phase 11 adds the media namespace `M_0`, faza11 §5.2). **Worked budget (corrected §19.23):** with a 3-slot ES every namespace's set covers the same three slots, so a funder of the maximum 9 channels spends 9 tokens per slot per week on them, leaving 16 − 9 − 2 = **5** per slot per week (4 with `M_0`) for the rotations it commits; beyond that a rotation waits for next week's tokens (L8-C6). After a slot-table change, each extra union relay costs one more token per funded namespace per week for up to 6 weeks (F9 §21.1). Members that are not funders spend **0** on channels.
- **Offline tolerance.** A funder online in week w registers `{w, w + 1}`; registrations live until `start(w + 2) + 1 h`, so deposits keep working for about two to three weeks after a funder's last renewal (F9 §21.2). Beyond that, stores wait in `wait_capability` and end at their deadlines (L8-C6).
- **Quota.** Every registration credits its capability's quota (256 MiB per week); any member (or invite holder) can exhaust it; the remedy is Remove and rotation (L8-C7).

### 7.5 `:sync` additions (normative; X-8)

```kotlin
/** A namespace this client funds: the planner redeems registration capabilities for it; stores and reads keep using bearer credentials. */
interface Funding {
    fun set(tx: SyncTransaction, namespace: NamespaceId, funded: Boolean)
    fun isFunded(tx: SyncTransaction, namespace: NamespaceId): Boolean
}
```

1. **`Funding`** and table `sync_namespace_fund`; `Capabilities.needed()` reports a `REGISTER` need (a new `CapabilityKind.REGISTER`) for a funded namespace with a pending registration row and no usable registration capability; the planner treats it like WRITE MISSING (redeem as soon as eligible).
2. **`relay_register_capability`** holds redeemed capabilities of funded namespaces; the registration step uses it (else, for Phase 9's owner namespaces, the write capability as today); store, list, get and check never read it.
3. **`SendDelay.OFF` refused for `Consumer.CHANNEL`** (a v6 trigger, the `sync_namespace_dm_delay_*` pattern).
4. No change to the listener and consumer-tail slots: the one messaging actor serves both consumers.
