5. `Inbox.defer` is used for unopenable channel blobs (F7's "an MLS message for a future epoch"); deferred rows do not count against `FETCHED_CAP` (`InboxStore.kt:156-165`).
6. **`Inbox.reofferDeferred(tx, namespace)`** (new; §19.1): sets `offer_after_minute = 0` on the namespace's deferred rows so the next `peek` returns them. Today `defer` only moves `offer_after_minute` and `peek` returns only due rows (`InboxStore.kt:332-360`), so a blob of epoch e + 1 deferred with a 6-hour backoff would wait out the backoff after e settles. Called from `CH-SETTLE`, `CH-JOIN` and `CH-SWITCH`; it causes no traffic.
7. **Registration rows of non-rotating funders** are created only by the funder renewal step at the EXPIRING renewal time (§6.5, §19.8); `Funding.set` for a new generation is called by the rotating committer's `CH-SWITCH` and by the renewal step, never by `CH-SETTLE` on a received commit.

### 7.6 `:entitlement` changes

The planner handles `REGISTER` needs as WRITE needs of a REDEEM namespace (the Q29 redeem hold arms for them); NI-K stays identical (its worlds have no channels). The T2 world (Phase 8) gains channel funders (§7.9).

### 7.7 When channel traffic happens (normative; T19, T40)

| Relay-visible event | Trigger | Timing |
|---|---|---|
| list, get | Phase 7 schedule per (relay, namespace) | T19, independent of activity |
| store of a post, reaction, reveal, `INVITE_ISSUED`, `LEAVE` | user action | at the first session where the channel is current, then STANDARD immediately or HIGH after U[0, 10 min] |
| store of a join or rejoin request | user action | as a post |
| store of a commit | committer pass or committer user action | CO-8 |
| store of its Welcomes | the committer's settle of its own winning commit (pass + 20 min + one listing pass) | CO-5, §19.2 |
| store of a self-update | PRF slot every 28 days; a retry at the next session after 24 h if it lost (LC-6) | CO-9 |
| store of a resignation, transfer, rejoin or restore request | user action | §6.6, §6.7, §6.12 |
| check | Phase 7 verification of own copies | Phase 7 |
| redeem, register | funder renewal (PRF, last 23 h of the week); rotating committer at the switch hour of its own commit | F8 §12.4, §6.5 |
| first list of a new generation's namespace | the commit's switch hour, or the next session start for a member that settled later or joined | §6.5, LC-4, LC-5 |
| **anything else, because a blob arrived** | **never** | T40 |

Declared: LC-6 (a pass commits only when work is pending; its Welcomes and a rotating commit's funding follow only if it won; a member's self-update is retried after 24 h only if it lost, CO-9), LC-4 and LC-5 (listening changes after a received rotation or Welcome, at commit-fixed or session-start times), the self-update retry after a lost tie-break (CO-9), and the catch-up-dependent store minute of posts composed offline (LC-7). **NFR-2** (p95 < 8 s): not met by design with the 30-second listing interval (the same reason as NFR-1, LIMITE L2.1 #5); measured in Phase 12 (Q21). **Read pairs (revised §19.8):** each channel costs 3 pairs, and at most 6 while a generation drains (at most one draining generation per channel, §6.5); with `maxReadPairs = 64` (`TrafficPolicy.kt:50`), the budget must hold `6 × channels + DM pairs + drop pairs ≤ 64` even when every channel drains at once — a hostile co-member who leaves every shared channel makes them all drain, and past the cap every pair, the DM inbox included, acts at 1/m of its events (`PairSchedule.kt:17-20,47`), a remotely triggered marker. **9 channels** per client keep that bound (54 pairs, leaving 10 for the DM inbox — 3, up to 6 after a slot-table change — and drops) (the P0 limit, Q4) **outside the six weeks after a slot-table change** (corrected §19.23): during that window readers list the union of the old and new sets (F9 §5.2), up to 6 relays per namespace after one change (more after two changes within six weeks), so a channel costs up to 6 pairs and up to 12 while it drains; 9 channels then take up to 54 pairs with no drain, and a single draining channel adds 6 more, which with the DM inbox's 3 … 6 pairs reaches 63 … 66 — at or past the cap — and a hostile co-member who leaves several shared channels inside that window pushes the client past the cap, where every pair, the DM inbox included, acts at 1/m of its events. The window follows an owner-controlled schedule change and affects every client alike; the residue is declared (L8-C24), and Phase 11's `M_0` (3 more pairs, faza11 §5.2) tightens it further.

### 7.8 Observables (T1)

No change to `allowed-observables.json` v2. Channel traffic uses `store` (hash, bucket, `ttl_bucket_seconds`, minute, the DEPOSIT `capability_scope`), `list`/`get`/`check` (the READ scope), and the funders' `redeem` and `register` (`key_kind` read or deposit). A new observable would be a privacy decision (INV:66); none is needed.

### 7.9 T2 world and gate (as F9 §4.9)

`t2-exit-gate` gains funders: world clients that own a channel fund it at the renewal time and at rotations they commit; the population line gains `channel_funded_namespaces`; the export is unchanged (no relay table is added). NI-1, NI-2, NI-3 and NI-K are unchanged in rule: no issuer input reaches channel events. **Ownership (corrected §19.23):** this is Rust work in `ghost/issuer/crates/service/tests/t2/world.rs` and belongs to **slice S6** (funding), sized there. **Dependency:** the nightly sweep of the T2 gate has never been green (NI-1d pairs 2 and 3, NI-1 across cells on pair 9; F9 §22.24, §22.28); Phase 10 adds no new failure mode but cannot make T2 green. A green T2 sweep is **not** a Phase 10 exit item; the channel funders' T2 run is reported with the sweep's existing state (Q39).

---

## 8. Ephemeral posts and the history policy

### 8.1 Timer, TTL, deadlines

The channel timer is `GhostChannelContext.timer_code` (set by the owner; default 7 d, ADR-27 point 7); a change is a GroupContextExtensions commit from rank 0 that every member processes and the UI shows ("the timer is itself a message shown to everyone", ADR-27 point 8). TTLs and deadlines: §5.8.

### 8.2 Countdown and deletion (ADR-32 points 4–7, applied to posts)

- The author's countdown starts at the commit of the `CH-POST` transaction that encrypted the post; a reader's at first display (`markDisplayed`).
- An unread post is deleted at `min(relay expiry E, consume + bucket + 1 h)`, E from `InboundBlob.relayExpiryHour`; deadlines only move earlier; the expiry clock never runs backwards.
- The cascade deletes, in one transaction: the post row, its reactions, its `ch_pending_post` row, the notification content, and every quote rendering (replies keep their own text; a `reply_to` that no longer resolves renders as expired); then `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` with `busy = 0` checked (the F9 `Checkpoint.truncate()`).
- Sweeps run where DM sweeps run (open, foreground sessions, the consumer tail, the in-process timer, the one inexact alarm of ADR-32); every channel read query filters expired rows (the F9 `DmExpiredRowLint` extended to `ch_*`).

### 8.3 Epoch-secret retention (the second meaning of "history policy"; spec §7.3, TM:83)

- Past epoch secrets (OpenMLS) and epoch keys `K_ep(e)` (GHOST) are kept until `final_day(e) + R` — `final_day(e)` being the day epoch e became past at this member (`ch_epoch.final_day`) — with `R = min(2 d, 2 × post_bucket + 1 h)` rounded up to whole days (1 d for 5 min, 1 h and 1 d channels; 2 d otherwise), and never more than 64 past epochs (`max_past_epochs(64)`). **Deletion is by count (§19.4):** SWEEP computes n = the number of past epochs whose `final_day + R ≥ today` (Kotlin's trusted day) and calls `delete_past_epoch_secrets(PastEpochDeletion::before_timestamp(UNIX_EPOCH).max_past_epochs(n))`, which drains the oldest past epochs beyond n (`past_secrets.rs:304-324`) and never consults OpenMLS's stamps; `K_ep` rows follow the same n. (Round 10a's `before_timestamp(day)` would have deleted every past epoch whenever the current epoch started before the cutoff, `past_secrets.rs:271-279`.)
- Why short: posts are encrypted at session (§6.3.1), so a post of epoch e reaches the relays within the send delay unless a relay outage delays its store; a longer retention only widens what a seized device decrypts (AD-8: relay-retained or recorded ciphertext of retained epochs, including posts the device never fetched; L8-C10).
- **Retention bounds past epochs only (corrected §19.19).** A seized device also holds the **current** epoch, whose sender ratchets derive forward up to 1 000 generations per sender (`sender_ratchet.rs:274-315`): every message of the current epoch the device has not consumed opens, from the epoch's start, whether fetched from an honest relay within its TTL or from a relay operator's recording (AD-1). The current epoch lasts until the next commit: at most about 7 days while a committer is active (the weekly PCS commit), about 1 day in channels with timers ≤ 1 d under Q36, and without limit while no committer commits (members' self-updates every 28 days shorten it in busy channels). Round 10b's "≤ 2 days" in L8-C10 was therefore wrong; in short-timer channels it also exceeded ADR-27's promise that a seized device holds "cel mult ultima fereastră de timp" of a conversation for anyone who recorded the relays.
- Generation secrets live while the generation is current or draining (§6.5).

### 8.4 History policy "none" (FR-3.8; D11)

A joiner holds no key of an epoch before its join epoch, so it opens no earlier post or commit (P-C5); it can list the namespace and see earlier blobs' timing and volume (metadata only, L8-C3). **It is not "no history" in every sense (corrected §19.22):** the joiner holds `JK_g` from its invite and opens the generation's earlier **join traffic** (other joiners' join requests: their KeyPackages, pseudonyms and invite ids, and Welcome blobs' outer layer), and it learns the generation number (how many rotations — removals among them — the channel has had), the epoch number and the tree's blank leaves. The UI therefore says "no content from before you joined", not "no history" (Q11's Romanian text is corrected). The policy byte is 0; values 1 and 2 are reserved and refused until an ADR defines a history transfer.

### 8.5 T25 for channels (cases; the EP suite of F9 §15.5 over channels)

Every timer × {displayed, unread with honest E, unread with short E, pending plaintext, post of a removed member, post on a losing fork branch, post arriving after its deadline}; a timer change mid-conversation (older posts keep their own timer, clamped by §5.5); a clock step back; a busy reader during checkpoint; the physical scan of the database and the WAL for a plaintext canary after expiry; T1 on the relay capture: the TTL is the bucket of §5.8 and nothing finer.

**Rows derived from posts (added §19.21).** Every row a post creates is either deleted with the post's timer or declared as membership metadata that outlives it:

| Row | Source | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `ch_reaction` | REACTION | cascades with its target post (ADR-32) |
| `ch_reveal` (both sides) | REVEAL | deleted with the REVEAL post's timer; no time column |
| `ch_invite_issued` | `INVITE_ISSUED` (an administrative post, handshake TTL, §5.8) | **declared** membership metadata: kept until `expiry_day + 1` (≤ 8 days), because committers admit up to the invite's expiry; it holds ids, keys and a signature, no user text (L8-C12) |
| `ch_request(leave)` | `LEAVE` (administrative) | **declared**: kept until the leave is served or `until_day` |
| `ch_sponsor` | the admitting commit's `GhostCommitAad` (not a post) | **declared** membership metadata for ADR-13 attribution: kept while the member is in the channel, deleted at its removal; its `admitted_day` feeds ADR-13's age weighting and is declared with it (T33, L8-C12) |

The EP-C suite runs these rows under every timer (a 5-minute channel's REVEAL row is gone after 5 minutes; its `ch_invite_issued` row after the invite's expiry); mutant ME-C5 (a derived row outliving its rule) is killed by EP-C and the physical scan.

---

## 9. Storage schema: migrations 6 and 7 (normative)

`CURRENT_VERSION` is 5 (`Schema.kt:120`). Phase 10 adds **migration 6** (the `:sync` half, slice S1) and **migration 7** (the channel tables, slice S3), each one transaction, append-only and fail-closed, following the v4/v5 split of F9 §13.6: explicit column lists, `typeof` CHECKs on integer columns, write-once and state-machine triggers, REPLACE guards (`replaceGuard`, `Schema.kt:1255-1258`), every time column `*_minute`, `*_hour` or `*_day` with its CHECK (T20, T33), no FTS (G-DM6 extended), no counters per channel except the bounded liveness counters of CO-11, no activity records except those declared (corrected §19.21; L8-C12): the committer's refresh days and last-activity epochs per member (§6.6, §6.7), its own last commit day (`ch_schedule.last_own_commit_day`, CO-8's PCS cadence), the liveness window's start minute and the last committer-commit day (CO-11, CO-12), the sponsor records' admission day (ADR-13 age weighting), and invite records until their expiry. `ch_epoch.anchor_minute` is cleared when the epoch becomes final, and `ch_reveal` has no time column but its deletion deadline.

**Migration 6 (`:sync`).**

| Object | Definition |
|---|---|
| `sync_namespace_fund` | `namespace_id BLOB PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES sync_namespace(namespace_id) ON DELETE CASCADE`, `WITHOUT ROWID`: presence = this client funds the namespace (§7.5). A side table because `sync_namespace.capability_source` has a frozen CHECK (`'redeem', 'local'`, `Schema.kt:852-853`) that SQLite cannot alter without rebuilding a referenced table |
| `relay_register_capability` | `(relay_id, namespace_id)` primary key, `token` (1..512 bytes), `expires_hour` (`% 3600 = 0`), `state IN ('usable','rejected','exhausted')`, `generation ≥ 1`; cascades from `relay_directory` and `sync_namespace`; `WITHOUT ROWID`; the `relay_capability` columns of v2 verbatim |
| triggers `sync_namespace_channel_delay_{insert,update}` | refuse `send_delay = 'off'` for `consumer = 'channel'` (the v4 DM triggers' text) |

**Migration 7 (channels).** Step 0: a guard table with `CHECK (row_count = 0)` fed by `count(*)` of each v1 table `channels`, `channel_pseudonyms`, `mls_state`, `posts` (no production code writes them: `ChannelPseudonymRepository` (`Repositories.kt:43-62`) has no caller outside tests, and its rows need a `channels` row that nothing writes; the guard fails closed if any row exists anyway; S3 deletes the repository and its tests), then `DROP INDEX idx_posts_channel` and the four `DROP TABLE`s. `media` and `media_blobs` stay for Phase 11. Step 1: the tables below. Secret-bearing columns are `*_env` (wrapped under the channel state key, §10.4); plaintext content columns are wrapped under the content key (F9 §13.8).

| Table | Key columns (all others NOT NULL unless marked) | Time columns | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ch_channel` | `channel_id` (32) PK; `state` ∈ {joining, active, leaving, left, removed, replaced, resync_needed, reinvite_needed, join_expired}; flags `split_suspected`, `partition_suspected`, `stale_suspected`, `clock_blocked` (0/1 each); `suite` = 3; `generation`; `epoch`; `joined_epoch`; `size_class` ∈ {0,1}; `timer_code` 0..5; `my_pseudonym` (32); `name_env` NULL | `last_committer_commit_day` NULL | the MLS state is in `mls_kv`, not here |
| `ch_liveness` | `channel_id` PK; `unopenable_count` 0..255; `unopenable_operators` (bitmask of operator slots); `sessions` 0..255; `progress_env` (the distinct progress senders, wrapped) | `unopenable_first_minute` NULL | CO-11, CO-12; reset at progress |
| `ch_generation` | (`channel_id`, `generation`) PK; `namespace_id` (32) UNIQUE; `state` ∈ {pending_switch, current, draining}; `g_env`, `jk_env`; `r_join` (32, a recognition key) | `switch_hour` NULL, `drain_until_day` NULL | at most one `current` and one `draining` per channel (triggers) |
| `ch_epoch` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`) PK; `state` ∈ {open, final}; `k_env`; `r_ep` (32); `winner_hash` (32) NULL; `own_seq` ≥ 0 (the `seq` of §5.6 for own posts) | `anchor_minute` NULL (a trigger requires it NULL once `state = final`, §19.21), `final_day` NULL, `keep_until_day` NULL | ≤ 65 rows per channel (trigger) |
| `ch_epoch_log` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`) PK; `winner_hash` (32) | `final_day` | ≤ 1 024 per channel; the rejoin fork check (§6.7) |
| `ch_candidate` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`, `hash`) PK; `sender` (32); `rank` 0..255; `own` 0/1; `verified` 0/1 (own commit: a copy verified, CO-4); `lifetime_pending` 0/1; `body_env`; `welcome_env` NULL (own commit only: its sealed WELCOME blobs, enqueued at settle if it wins); `box_env` NULL (own commit only: the new leaf's box key, moved to `ch_box_key` if it wins) | `first_seen_minute`, `store_hour` NULL | ≤ 8 per epoch plus the own commit; one per sender; deleted when their epoch settles (the own commit also when CO-4 clears it) |
| `ch_fork_signal` | (`channel_id`, `epoch`, `hash`) PK | `first_seen_day` | ≤ 16 per channel; diagnostics only (CO-6) |
| `mls_kv` | (`channel_id`, `k`) PK; `v_env` | — | `WITHOUT ROWID`; written only from change-sets |
| `mls_global_kv` | `k` PK; `v_env`; `channel_id` (32), the channel the row was written for (§3.4) | `delete_after_day` NULL | KeyPackage and key-pair rows; deleted with the channel or by day |
| `ch_post` | `op_id` (16) PK; `channel_id`; `direction` ∈ {out, in}; `author` (32); `kind` ∈ {1, 2}; `reply_to` (16) NULL; `timer_code`; `epoch`; `body_env`; `state` ∈ {queued, sent, degraded, failed, indeterminate, unread, read} | `author_minute` (`% 60 = 0`), `displayed_minute` NULL, `delete_at_minute` NULL, `relay_expiry_hour` NULL | the expiry filter in every read query |
| `ch_reaction` | (`target`, `author`) PK; `channel_id`; `grapheme_env` | — | cascades from `ch_post` |
| `ch_pending_post` | `op_id` PK; `channel_id`; `kind`; `content_env` | `fail_after_hour` | encrypt-at-session queue (§6.3.1) |
| `ch_outbound_op` | `operation_id` PK; `channel_id`; `purpose` ∈ {post, commit, welcome, join, rejoin, request}; `ref` NULL | — | maps Phase 7 ops to channel objects |
| `ch_join` | (`channel_id`, `kp_ref`) PK; `type` ∈ {join, rejoin, restore}; `invite_id` (16) NULL; `generation` | `expiry_day` | invitee, rejoiner or restorer side; the KeyPackage's private keys live in `mls_global_kv` until `expiry_day` (7 days for a rejoin) |
| `ch_join_request` | (`channel_id`, `hash`) PK; `generation`; `type` ∈ {join, rejoin, restore}; `invite_id` NULL; `body_env` | `until_day` | committer side; ≤ 64 per channel |
| `ch_invite_issued` | (`channel_id`, `invite_id`) PK; `invite_pk` (32); `sponsor` (32); `sponsor_sig` (64); `consumed` 0/1; `void` 0/1 (sponsor removed, §19.7) | `expiry_day` | from `INVITE_ISSUED` posts; consumed and void set at settle |
| `ch_kp_seen` | (`channel_id`, `tag`) PK, `tag` = `HMAC(local key, kp_ref ‖ init_key)[0..16]` | `until_day` | committers only; ≤ 1 024 per channel; `WITHOUT ROWID` (§19.3) |
| `ch_member_seen` | (`channel_id`, `member`) PK; `last_epoch` | — | committers only: the last epoch with an authenticated message from the member's current leaf (§6.7; L8-C12) |
| `ch_invite_own` | `invite_id` PK; `channel_id`; `invite_env` | `expiry_day` | the sponsor's link, for the UI |
| `ch_sponsor` | (`channel_id`, `member`) PK; `sponsor`; `invite_id` | `admitted_day` | ADR-13 attribution |
| `ch_member_refresh` | (`channel_id`, `member`) PK | `refresh_day` | committers only (§6.6) |
| `ch_request` | (`channel_id`, `kind`, `subject`) PK; `kind` ∈ {leave, remove, policy, class} ; `payload_env` NULL | `until_day` | a committer's pending work |
| `ch_schedule` | `channel_id` PK | `next_selfupdate_day`, `last_own_commit_day` NULL, `next_pass_minute` NULL | CO-8, CO-9 |
| `ch_seen` | (`channel_id`, `tag`) PK, `tag` = `HMAC(local key, op_id)[0..16]` | `until_day` | `WITHOUT ROWID`; the `dm_seen` pattern |
| `ch_reveal` | (`channel_id`, `direction`, `peer`) PK | `delete_at_minute` (the REVEAL post's timer deadline; no other time, §19.17) | the card goes to `dm_contact` through `addContact` with source `link` |
| `ch_box_key` | (`channel_id`, `box_pk`) PK; `sk_env`; `last_epoch` NULL (NULL while the key is in the member's current leaf; set when a new leaf replaces it) | — | the member's own box keys (§5.1, §19.17); deleted by SWEEP when `last_epoch` leaves retention (§8.3) |

`expectedTables` and `expectedTriggers` gain the new names and lose the four v1 tables; `DmSchemaIntrospectionTest`'s rule (every time column checked, no virtual table) extends to `ch_*` and `mls_*` (`ChannelSchemaIntrospectionTest`).

## 10. Modules, the closed JNI surface, the facade (normative)

### 10.1 Module layout

| Module / package | Contents | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `client-core/mls` (Rust, `ghost-client-mls`; new) | `GhostKv` (§3.4), `PseudonymSigner`, the channel operations over `MlsGroup`, the seal (§5.6), derivations (§5.2), `ChannelContent`, invite and join codecs, the reveal HPKE and the per-leaf box keys, result codes, the JNI export behind `jni-bridge`; no network, async runtime, logger or clock; `rayon` pinned to one worker | `openmls`, `openmls_traits`, `openmls_libcrux_crypto`, `ed25519-dalek` 2.2.0, `hkdf`, `hmac`, `sha2`, `chacha20poly1305`, `serde_json`, `zeroize`; `ghost-client-dm` (identity signing for reveals, the card); Phase 11's `ghost-client-media` only with the channel-media slice (§5.10) |
| `client-core/net` | `ghost_client_mls::export_jni!()` beside the DM export (`net/src/jni_bridge.rs:63` pattern) | `ghost-client-mls` |
| `client-core/dm-host` (`ghost-dm-host`) | also exports `MlsNative` (with a `test-vectors` entry), so JVM tests load one host library; the host build's process-global seeded randomness (§12.1, §19.10) | `ghost-client-mls` |
| `client-core/mls-kat` (`ghost-client-mls-kat`; new, test-only, outside every shipped and host library) | the WG passive-client replay through `GhostKv` (§12.2, V10-12) | `ghost-client-mls`; `openmls` with `test-utils` as a dev-dependency |
| `:crypto-bridge` `org.ghost.crypto.bridge` | `MlsNative` (§10.2), `MlsResult`, `ChannelCrypto` (typed facade), `ChannelRecognition` (hint matching with recognition keys), `ChannelInvite` codec wrapper; its types are byte arrays and its own value types | `libghost_client_net.so` only — **no project dependency** (corrected §19.23): the module has none today and its build file forbids `:storage` and `:messaging` (`ghost/android/crypto-bridge/build.gradle.kts`), and `:sync` api-exports `:storage` and `:network` (`ghost/android/sync/build.gradle.kts:31-32`), which would put network classes on the bridge's classpath against G-DM1 |
| `:messaging` `…messaging.channel.engine` | the transactions of §10.4 run by the **messaging actor** (the DM actor, renamed in code comments only), `ChannelPeekOrder`, `CommitArbiter` (CO rules), `Generations`, `Funders`, `ChannelSweeper`, the channel consumer steps; the mapping between the bridge's values and `:sync` types (namespace ids, credentials) | `:crypto-bridge`, `:sync`, `:identity` |
| `…messaging.channel.store` | DAOs over `ch_*`, `mls_*` (every read applies the expiry filter) | `:storage`, `:sync` API |
| `…messaging.api` | `Channels` facade (§10.3), value types with redacted `toString` | — |
| `:storage` | migrations 6 and 7 | — |
| `:sync` | §7.5 | — |
| `:entitlement` | §7.6 | `:sync` |
| `:app` | wiring: the actor already holds the listener and the tail; the invite deep link `ghost://channel/` is Phase 13 UI | all |

Rules: `:messaging` and `:crypto-bridge` reference no network class and no coroutine (G-DM1 extended); only `:crypto-bridge` references `MlsNative` (G-M6); typed catches only (`sync-no-catch-all.sh` scope); no protocol logic in Kotlin beyond orchestration (ADR-19): parsing, sealing, derivations and every MLS operation are native.

### 10.2 `MlsNative`: the closed list (gate G-M6)

All functions are `static native`, take byte arrays and ints, return `code(1) ‖ TLV fields`, perform no I/O, read no clock (a `day` or `minute` input is supplied by Kotlin), log nothing, and run under `catch_unwind` with the silent panic hook (`INTERNAL`). `rows` is the TLV array of wrapped `mls_kv`/`mls_global_kv` rows of §3.4; every state-changing function returns a change-set.

| # | Function | Main outputs | Codes besides `OK` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ctxOpen(stateKey32)`, `ctxClose(h)` | handle | `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE` |
| 2 | `channelOpen(h, channelId, pseudonymSeed32)`, `channelClose(h, channelId)` | P (Kotlin compares P with `:identity`'s value) | `INVALID_KEY` |
| 3 | `groupCreate(h, channelId, policy, day)` | change-set, `K_ep(0)` env, `R_ep(0)`, `G_0` env, generation values (fn 13) | `INVALID_POLICY` |
| 4 | `keyPackage(h, channelId, purpose, day)` | KeyPackage bytes (lifetime `[day − 4, day + 80]`, `GhostKpBind`, a fresh `box_pk`), `kp_ref`, global change-set, the new box key wrapped (`box_env`, §19.17) | — |
| 5 | `welcomeJoin(h, channelId, rows, welcomeBlobBody, day, expectedNs)` (`rows`: for a rejoin or restore, only the KeyPackage's global rows, §6.2.5) | change-set, context fields, roster, epoch, `K_ep` env, bootstrap (g, `G_g` env), authenticated (GroupInfo signature or `K_boot(e)`) and checked against `expectedNs` when its generation matches (§5.4) — through `JoinBuilder::skip_lifetime_validation()` and GHOST's structural leaf-lifetime check (§19.4) | `NOT_FOR_ME`, `INVALID_WELCOME`, `INVALID_BOOTSTRAP`, `INVALID_POLICY`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 6 | `receive(h, channelId, rows, epoch, mlsBytes, day)` (`rows` may be stored rows composed with a trial change-set, CO-3) | `APP`: content, sender P, the `ChannelOrder` checked against the MLS sender, change-set; `COMMIT`: a validation summary (sender and rank inputs, adds, removes, swaps, the parsed and verified `GhostCommitAad` evidence, context change, rotation flag, the update-path leaf's `refresh_epoch`, signature key and credential checks, size, hash, the staged `epoch_authenticator`), **no change-set**; `PROPOSAL`: nothing (dropped unprocessed, §6.3.2); `OWN`: nothing (an `OwnPrivateMessage` result, evidence of nothing, §6.3.2) | `INVALID_COMMIT(reason)`, `LIFETIME_PENDING`, `UNDECRYPTABLE`, `WRONG_EPOCH`, `ORDER_MISMATCH`, `NON_CANONICAL`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 7 | `settle(h, channelId, rows, winnerBytes or OWN, day, trial)` | change-set (wrapped; **not to be written** when `trial`), new epoch, `K_ep` env, `R_ep`, rotation flag and, if set, `G_{g+1}` env and generation values, the commit's `commit_minute`, roster diff, `epoch_authenticator` | `INVALID_COMMIT`, `LIFETIME_PENDING`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 8 | `clearOwn(h, channelId, rows)` | change-set | `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 9 | `commit(h, channelId, rows, adds[(kp, evidence)], removes[P], swaps[(P, kp, evidence)], contextUpdate?, rotate, selfUpdateOnly, floor, commitMinute, day)` | commit bytes (with `GhostCommitAad` set through `MlsGroup::set_aad`, `om:src/group/mls_group/mod.rs:329`, and the update-path `GhostLeaf.refresh_epoch` and fresh `box_pk` through `leaf_node_parameters`; never `build_with_new_signer`, §19.14), Welcome bytes (kept by Kotlin until settle), joiners' `kp_ref`s, bootstrap handling, the items kept and deferred (§5.7 rule), the new box key wrapped (`box_env`, kept in the own candidate row until the commit wins), change-set (pending commit) | `NO_FIT`, `INVALID_KEYPACKAGE(index)`, `INVALID_EVIDENCE(index)`, `INVALID_POLICY` |
| 10 | `encrypt(h, channelId, rows, content, seq, day)` (`seq` = `ch_epoch.own_seq`, incremented in the same transaction; the call sets `ChannelOrder` as the message's MLS AAD, §5.5) | MLS bytes, `sender_leaf`, change-set | `PENDING_COMMIT` (only when the own pending commit removes a pseudonym, §6.3.1, §19.13), `STATE_CORRUPT`. The channel-media slice adds `dmHandle` and `mediaKeyEnv` (§5.10); not in P0's list |
| 11 | `seal(h, keyEnv, keyClass, ns, bucket, mode, order, body)` | blob | `NO_FIT` |
| 12 | `open(h, keyEnv, keyClass, ns, mode, blob)` | order, body, and for mode 0x11 the PrivateMessage header's epoch, content type (to classify late candidates, CO-6, and to drop proposals) and, for an application message, its `ChannelOrder` | `NOT_OPEN`, `NON_CANONICAL` (including an `order` that differs from the header epoch or the `ChannelOrder`, CS-S7) |
| 13 | `generation(h, gEnv, slots[])` | `ns`, per-slot READ and DEPOSIT credentials v3 and verifiers, `JK` env, `R_join` | — |
| 14 | `inviteBuild(h, channelId, gEnv, invite fields)`, `inviteParse(bytes)` | invite bytes and `INVITE_ISSUED` fields with `sponsor_sig` / parsed fields | `NON_CANONICAL`, `UNSUPPORTED_SUITE` |
| 15 | `joinRequest(h, invite, kp)`, `rejoinRequest(h, channelId, generation, day, lastEpoch, lastHash, kp)`, `joinVerify(h, channelId, body, invitePk?, memberP?)` | body / KeyPackage, `kp_ref`, type and verified evidence | `BAD_SIGNATURE`, `INVALID_KEYPACKAGE`, `WRONG_PURPOSE` |
| 16 | `revealSeal(h, dmHandle, channelId, recipientP, recipientBoxPk, minute, opId)`, `revealOpen(h, channelId, boxEnv, enc, ct, opId, senderP)` (`boxEnv`: the member's box key of the post's epoch, `ch_box_key`) | TLV values / verified card and bindings | `NOT_OPEN`, `BAD_SIGNATURE` |
| 17 | `contentEncode`, `contentDecode` | canonical `ChannelContent` | `NON_CANONICAL` |
| 18 | `roster(h, channelId, rows)` | members (P, leaf, `box_pk`, `refresh_epoch`), epoch, `epoch_authenticator`, tree hash | `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 19 | `sweepPast(h, channelId, rows, keepCount)` | change-set (`before_timestamp(UNIX_EPOCH).max_past_epochs(keepCount)`, §8.3) | `STATE_CORRUPT` |

`MlsResultCodesTest` enumerates the codes; G-M7 includes a compile-time exhaustive `match` over OpenMLS's error enums used, so a variant added at a bump fails the build (F9 G-R7(d)).

### 10.3 `Channels` facade for Phase 13 (normative signatures)

```kotlin
interface Channels {
    fun create(name: String, timer: Timer, sizeClass: SizeClass): CreateResult              // CREATED | LIMIT_REACHED | NO_REPLICAS | CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE
    fun createInvite(channel: ChannelId, expiryDays: Int): InviteResult                    // link + QR bytes | NOT_ALLOWED | LIMIT_REACHED
    fun acceptInvite(link: ByteArray): AcceptResult                                        // PENDING | INVALID | EXPIRED | UNSUPPORTED_SUITE | ALREADY_MEMBER | LIMIT_REACHED
    fun channels(): List<ChannelView>                                                      // id, name, state, role, member count; no times
    fun posts(channel: ChannelId, limit: Int, before: PostCursor?): List<PostView>
    fun post(channel: ChannelId, text: String, replyTo: PostId?): PostResult               // QUEUED | TOO_LARGE | NOT_MEMBER | STORAGE_FULL | CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE
    fun react(target: PostId, grapheme: String?): PostResult
    fun markDisplayed(ids: List<PostId>)
    fun members(channel: ChannelId): List<MemberView>                                     // pseudonym fingerprint, role, sponsor; no times
    fun remove(channel: ChannelId, member: PseudonymId): Result                            // committers only
    fun leave(channel: ChannelId): Result                                                  // NOT_ALLOWED for a committer until it resigned or transferred (§6.6)
    fun setPolicy(channel: ChannelId, change: PolicyChange): Result                        // owner: timer, name, moderators, funders, class, invites, transfer of rank 0; any committer: resign
    fun reveal(channel: ChannelId, member: PseudonymId): Result                            // after the Phase 13 warning
    fun resync(channel: ChannelId): Result                                                 // rejoin request
    fun fingerprint(channel: ChannelId): Fingerprint                                       // (epoch, 8 bytes of epoch_authenticator), for out-of-band comparison (CO-12)
    fun hide(post: PostId); fun mute(channel: ChannelId, muted: Boolean)                   // FR-3.6: local only
    fun status(): ChannelStatus                                                            // counts only
    fun setListener(listener: ChannelListener?)                                            // content-free change hints
}
```

Every method posts to the messaging actor and returns after its transaction commits; none performs network I/O.

### 10.4 Transactions (normative inventory; each is one SQLCipher transaction, change-sets applied only on OK)

| Name | Trigger | Writes |
|---|---|---|
| `CH-CREATE` | user | channel, generation 0, epoch 0, `mls_kv`, `ch_box_key`, namespace, credentials, funding, registrations |
| `CH-INVITE` | user | `ch_invite_own`, `INVITE_ISSUED` post and op |
| `CH-ACCEPT` | user | `ch_channel(joining)`, `ch_join`, `mls_global_kv`, namespace, credentials, join op |
| `CH-JOIN` | a WELCOME blob | group rows, channel, generation (a newer bootstrap generation as `pending_switch`), epoch; deletes join state; `reofferDeferred`; **no** namespace, outbox, registration, funding or capability call (G-M5) |
| `CH-POST` | user or the channel becoming current | post row, op; or `ch_pending_post` |
| `CH-RECV` | a claimed blob (one per blob, in peek order) | post or reaction rows, `mls_kv` change-set for application messages, candidates, join requests, invite records, reveal records, fork signals, liveness counters and flags (CO-11, CO-12); `markConsumed` or `defer` |
| `CH-PASS` | CO-8, CO-9, a committer's user action | pending commit (`mls_kv`), the commit op, `ch_candidate(own)` with the sealed Welcomes wrapped in `welcome_env` (not enqueued) |
| `CH-SETTLE` | time (the CO-4 settle point), outcome of own commit | merge (after trial settles when CO-3 needs them; their change-sets are never written), epoch rows and log (`anchor_minute` cleared), the own winning commit's Welcome ops and its new box key into `ch_box_key` (the replaced key's `last_epoch` set), consumed and void invites, sponsor, refresh and member-seen rows, the next generation as `pending_switch`, `reofferDeferred` |
| `CH-SWITCH` | the first listing step at or after `switch_hour`, or the next session start (§19.8) | namespaces (register the new, mark the old draining, end an older drain), credentials, the rotating committer's funding; `reofferDeferred` |
| `CH-REJOIN` | user | KeyPackage rows, `ch_join(rejoin)`, the signed rejoin op(s) |
| `CH-REVEAL` | user | reveal post and op, `ch_reveal` |
| `CH-LEAVE` | user | `LEAVE` post and op, state |
| `CH-OUTCOME` | Phase 7 outcomes | post states, own-commit bookkeeping |
| `CH-SWEEP` | the ADR-32 sweep points | expired posts and their derived rows (§8.5), epochs past retention (`sweepPast` with the count of §8.3) and the box keys whose `last_epoch` left it, drained generations (their still-deferred fetched rows marked consumed first, §6.5), expired joins, invites, requests, seen tags, KeyPackage tags, fork signals; an own INDETERMINATE commit past its deadline plus the handshake TTL (CO-4 (c)); checkpoint |

**State key.** `mls_state_key`: 32 random bytes created at the first channel use, wrapped by a non-exportable Keystore key with the database key's authentication policy (the F9 §2.5 `session_state_key` pattern; one key for all channels), held in native memory while unlocked (L7-D19 extended). A restore creates a new one (channel state is lost anyway).

## 11. Invariants (new T36–T41; changes to existing ones)

### 11.1 New invariants (Romanian rows for INVARIANTS.md in Appendix B.1)

| ID | Invariant | Verification | Executable from |
|---|---|---|---|
| **T36** | Channel blindness at relays: no relay-visible value is computable from `channel_id`, pseudonym or box keys, KeyPackages, invite ids or MLS headers without a generation, epoch or invite secret; every channel blob is a §5.6 seal; no MLS header byte outside it | derivation vectors; G-M3 (static); JC-1 (every relay-visible value of the world recomputed from public material: 0 matches), JC-2 (header scan of every stored blob for group id, epoch, KeyPackageRef, pseudonym keys: 0); PC-1, PC-7, PC-13 | Phase 10 (S2, S10) |
| **T37** | One scope per kind and uniform shape: every store into a generation namespace at a relay presents that (relay, namespace)'s DEPOSIT credential, every list/get its READ credential, registration capabilities appear only in `register`; in channels with timer 7 d, 30 d or off, every blob has the channel TTL, and every commit has the floor bucket | JC-3, JC-4; NI-CH2; PC-3, PC-4, PC-8, PC-10 | Phase 10 (S10) |
| **T38** | Removal: nothing a removed member held (retained epoch and generation secrets, credentials, KeyPackage keys) opens a blob of an epoch after the removing commit, it holds no secret of any later generation, and no post composed by an honest member after that member first held the removing candidate opens under the removed member's keys (the removal hold, CO-13); the same for the stack a **restore** replaced (§19.16); all for members to which the relays delivered the removing commit (§19.15) | the removed-member seizure oracle over every later blob, its hold variant over every post composed after its author's first sighting of the removing commit, and its restore variant over the replaced stack (every blob of every later generation, not only later epochs); PC-2, PC-14, MM-7, MM-8, MM-29 | Phase 10 (S6, S10) |
| **T39** | Joiner history "none": nothing a joiner holds opens a blob of an epoch before its join epoch; the generation's earlier join traffic (mode 0x12 and the outer layer of 0x13), which it opens with the invite's `JK_g`, is the declared exception (L8-C3, §19.22) | the joiner oracle (with that exception counted and reported); PC-2 | Phase 10 (S4, S10) |
| **T40** | No reflex in channels: two worlds that differ only in what a client received give identical relay views of that client (stores, redeems, registrations, list schedule, session close times), except the declared LC-6 (pass outcome; Welcomes and a rotating commit's funding after a won commit; a self-update retry after a lost one), LC-4 (a received rotation changes the listened namespaces at the commit's switch hour or the next session start) and LC-5 (a joiner's listening after its Welcome) | NI-CH1 (valid, forged, replayed, join request, commit, rejoin request), NI-CH4 (rotating vs non-rotating commit); G-M5 (static: the enqueue, register, redeem and namespace call sites); PC-5, PC-6, PC-15. Session close times are compared only once the world models circuit timing (§19.10); until then the report names the parts of the view compared | Phase 10 (S5, S11) |
| **T41** | Convergence: members whose system clocks are within the KeyPackage lifetime margin and that hold the same valid candidates and the same held blobs for epoch e when they settle it merge the same commit, and equal `epoch_authenticator`s follow at every epoch; commit validity depends only on the commit, the group state and its authenticated data; a settled epoch is never left because of a later commit; a member on a dead branch reaches `resync_needed` or `reinvite_needed`; branches kept apart by the relays, and a member cut off in silence, are flagged (`split_suspected`, `partition_suspected`, `stale_suspected`) and are not claimed to be detected in-band; every leaf a commit installs keeps its member's signature key and credential; all of this while at least one relay serving each member delivers every commit within its TTL | EM-3 (with the §19.1 and §19.13 adversaries); SCN-M20 and its FORK and owner-restore variants; SCN-CL, SCN-CM; `oracle_epoch`; MM-2, MM-3, MM-4, MM-16, MM-19 … MM-28, MM-31 … MM-34, MM-36, MM-37 | Phase 10 (S5, S7) |

### 11.2 Changes to existing invariants

| ID | Change |
|---|---|
| T1 | no schema change; channel traffic fixtures added to `privacy-capture.sh` (stores with deposit scopes of channel shape, registrations by funders) |
| T3 | canaries extended: pseudonym seeds, box keys, `G_g`, `K_ep`, `JK_g`, invite seeds, KeyPackage private keys, post text, channel names; G-M4 over the MLS graph |
| T9 | channels: bucket = §5.7 (floor by class, never by type), bytes uniform (CS-S1) |
| T13 | channels: `author_minute` in `ChannelContent`; KeyPackage lifetimes day-aligned |
| T14 | unchanged (vectors); the "no rotation on re-join" reading of ADR-04 is recorded (X-4) |
| T20 | the new time columns of migrations 6 and 7 join the introspection sets |
| T21 | unchanged: channel namespaces use their circuit; registration capabilities pass through the existing matrix |
| T25 | extended to channels: the §8.5 cases |
| T27 | amended (§19.22): "a namespace that is not its own" becomes "a namespace that is neither its own nor a channel namespace it funds"; "redemptions and registrations do not depend on DM activity" gains "nor on channel activity, except the rotating committer's funding of the generation its own won commit opened (LC-3, LC-6)" |
| T28, T31 | T28's wording covers DMs; T40 is its channel twin. T31 already allows reading "canalele"; an invitee reads the invite's namespace until expiry; T31's "own writes at activity-independent times" gains the same exception as T27 (a funder's renewal is activity-independent; a rotating committer's funding follows its own commit) (§19.22) |
| T33 | extended: channel tables; OpenMLS state holds no sub-day time (day rewrite, day-aligned lifetimes), verified by a state scan over every stored `mls_kv` value (ME-C-series mutants); the activity-time columns it allows are exactly those §9 declares (§19.21), and the introspection test lists them (a new time column fails it) |

## 12. Test plan and exit-gate argument

**Lessons from Phase 9 built in (testing research §2).** L-1: counts (seeds, mutants) are pinned only after S0 measures the cost, and a mutant is registered only once its killer exists. L-2 (revised §19.11): CI restoration (the artifact quota, a KVM runner) is a precondition of the S10 and S11 exits, where the gates first run, not of S0 — S0's checks run locally. L-3: the runner budget is 4 runners on the owner's laptop, running only while he is logged in (`docs/ci-self-hosted.md` §1); gate jobs run nightly and on dispatch, and S0 measures the total nightly occupancy before the channel gates are added. L-4: exact joins and twins carry the privacy proof; statistics over a saturated LC attacker prove nothing, so Phase 10 has none. L-5: every property needs a mutant that can falsify it.

### 12.1 Infrastructure: `ChannelWorld`

- **Clients:** 2–24 per world (60 in the privacy world), each a real `:messaging` + `:crypto-bridge` + `:sync` stack on its own SQLite file (production pragmas, WAL and DELETE journal modes), migrated to v7, driven by the Phase 7 `DeterministicDriver`, sharing the DM harness through the shared-test-source pattern of F9 §15.1.
- **Real OpenMLS** through `MlsNative` loading `ghost-dm-host` (which now links `ghost-client-mls`). **Determinism (corrected §19.10):** the seeded `getrandom` backend F9 designed was **never built** (F9 §22 item 13; l.3862), and OpenMLS draws HPKE randomness from `SysRng` on the `rayon` worker thread (`treekem.rs:75`, `hpke-rs-libcrux/src/lib.rs:355-369`), so the per-actor-thread stream F9 described cannot attribute those draws to a world. S2 builds a **process-global** ChaCha20 stream keyed by the world seed behind getrandom's custom backend (`--cfg getrandom_backend="custom"`, host build only, G-R2), with every native call of a JVM serialised through one lock so the draw order is the call order; **S0 check V10-6** requires two runs of a scripted world to produce byte-identical commits, Welcomes and blob hashes. If V10-6 fails, the harness records and replays native outputs from the start, and crash enumeration uses the recording (sized in §14).
- **Virtual time anchored to the host clock (§19.4).** OpenMLS checks KeyPackage lifetimes against the host's `SystemTime::now()`, which no world can move. Worlds therefore run in the past: a world's first virtual day is at least `host day − 79` and its last at most `host day + 3`, so every KeyPackage made on a virtual day d (lifetime `[d − 4, d + 80]`) is valid on the host clock. SCN-M20's ≈ 80 days fit exactly; longer worlds are refused by the harness. Expiry cases (an expired invite, an expired KeyPackage at a committer) are tested by GHOST's own day checks; OpenMLS's own lifetime site is tested in Rust with KeyPackages built relative to the host clock (V10-11, the `clock_blocked` cases).
- **`ModelRelay`** (conformance-pinned by `relay_semantics.txt` and `register.txt`) with the Phase 7 and 9 hostile modes plus: **FORK** (serves different same-epoch commits to two subsets), **WITHHOLD_COMMIT** (hides one commit from a subset for a chosen time, or for good), **REORDER_ACROSS_EPOCH** (application messages after the next commit), **RESURRECT_COMMIT**, **DROP_WELCOME**, **EXPIRE_LOG** (drops handshake blobs before an offline member returns), **FLOOD_SELFUPDATE** (a member's modified client floods rank-255 commits), **COPY_JOIN** (a join request copied to another namespace), **LATE_COMMIT** (a valid commit for an epoch stored after the others settled, of a better rank), **GRIND** (a modified member client keeps an old epoch's state and stores a hash-ground self-update for a settled epoch), **SPAM_UNOPENABLE** (a member or invite holder stores random blobs in the current namespace, or a member removed at the end of the old generation in the draining one), **REPLAY_JOIN** (an insider re-wraps an earlier KeyPackage as a rejoin or restore), and, added by §19.13 … §19.18: **RESEAL_ORDER** (a member re-seals another member's post under a false `order`), **PROPOSE** (a member stores an MLS proposal), **FORGE_OWN** (a member forges sender data so that a blob claims the victim's own leaf), **REPLACE_BOOTSTRAP** (a holder of `JK_g` re-seals a WELCOME with its own bootstrap), **NEW_SIGNER** (a member's modified client commits an update path with a new signature key or a mismatched credential), **SILENT_ORPHAN** (EXPIRE_LOG on a rotating commit for one member, after which its old namespace goes quiet), and the Phase 9 harness debts this phase builds (§19.10): the redeem lane inside the world, send delays with HIGH mode (`not_before_minute` honoured), circuit open and close times, refusals.
- **Faults:** `FaultySqlExecutor` at every statement, pre- and post-commit, `SQLITE_FULL` injection; `SimulatedNativeCrash` inside `receive`, `settle` (real and trial), `commit` and `welcomeJoin`; a virtual clock with steps.
- **Oracles:** `oracle_epoch(client, epoch, winner_hash, epoch_authenticator, tree_hash)`; `oracle_displayed(client, op_id)` with a primary key; the **reload oracle** (§3.4 point 6); `oracle_seizure(removed client)` and `oracle_joiner(client)` (§12.8); `oracle_traffic(client, store | redeem | register, cause)` written by every facade call and every scheduled step, so NI-CH1 and G-M5 can map every relay-visible act to a non-inbound cause.

### 12.2 Unit, vector, property and interoperability tests

- **Rust (`cargo test -p ghost-client-mls`, `rust` job):** derivation vectors `ch_derive.txt`; seal vectors `ch_seal.txt` (every mode and bucket, hints, copy and truncation refused); `ch_content.txt`, `ch_invite.txt`, `ch_join.txt`; `GhostKvScopeTest` (all 72 storage methods mapped); the day rewrite (golden `MessageSecrets`); change-set atomicity (a call that fails after partial writes returns nothing); `rayon` single worker; the admission checks of §6.2.4 (each refusal); CO-2 validity (each rule); property tests with 10⁶ seeded inputs per CI run for every GHOST parser (never panics, never over-reads).
- **Kotlin (`android` job):** an independent JDK implementation replays `ch_derive.txt`, `ch_seal.txt` (outer layer and hints), `ch_content.txt` and `ch_invite.txt`; `MlsResult` exhaustiveness; migrations 6 and 7 (introspection, triggers, guards, REPLACE refusals); the `Funding` planner rule; the credential selection (a registration capability never reaches store, list or get).
- **RFC 9420 interoperability fixtures (spec Appendix A; revised §19.10).** (1) The MLS WG test vectors, as OpenMLS carries them in `openmls/test_vectors/` (`passive-client-welcome`, `passive-client-handling-commit`, `passive-client-random`, `tree-validation`, `treekem`, `message-protection`, `welcome`, `deserialization`, `storage-stability` and the others), run by OpenMLS's own KAT tests in `mls-vendor-tests` — **a git checkout of the openmls repository at commit `3a3e35de3feeca8f6605143c464d5452ae584d43`** (tag `openmls-v0.9.0`, the commit the crates.io package was built from, `.cargo_vcs_info.json`), pinned by hash: the crates.io package ships only `benches`, `examples`, `src` and `tests`, while the KATs read `../test_vectors/*.json`. (2) GHOST replays the three passive-client files **through `GhostKv` and the value-semantics call sequence** in a separate test-only crate `ghost-client-mls-kat` (outside every shipped graph, G-M2) with `openmls/test-utils` as a dev-dependency, because injecting the vectors' private keys needs `KeyPackageBundle::new` (`om:src/key_packages/mod.rs:752-756`, `test-utils` only). OpenMLS's own replay switches off lifetime validation through a crate-internal flag (`passive_client.rs:110,195`; "Some KATs use key packages that are expired by now", `validation.rs:875-881`) that no other crate can set, so GHOST's replay runs **only the vectors and epochs whose KeyPackages are valid on the host clock** and reports which (S0 check V10-12); it must reproduce every `epoch_authenticator` of those. The vector files are committed with their SHA-256 and the upstream commit. Until V10-12 passes, item (2) does not count towards spec Appendix A. (3) An optional differential peer (mls-rs, test-only; Q22).

### 12.3 EM-3: the exhaustive commit-arbitration model (the F9 EM-1 method)

An abstract model of CO-1 … CO-14 and §6.5–§6.7 in Rust, enumerated exhaustively: 2 committers (ranks 0 and 1), 2 members, epochs ≤ 4, message loss, duplication and reordering, a partition of either side for a chosen interval or for good, settle timers with store-hour anchors, process death at every step, one removal, one join and one rejoin. **Adversaries (added by §19.1):** a late but better commit (stored after every honest member settled); a malicious member that keeps an old epoch's state and grinds a self-update below a settled winner; a returning member that catches up across several epochs with and without successors on each candidate; a member flooding self-updates; a member or invite holder storing unopenable blobs; a relay withholding a removing commit from a subset of two members for good; a replayed KeyPackage re-wrapped as a rejoin or restore; a member whose system clock rejects a lifetime; **and (added by §19.13 … §19.15)** a committer whose own commit ends INDETERMINATE while others merge it, the owner on a dead branch (it must be readmitted), a resignation and a transfer of rank 0 followed by the former owner's leave, a moderator and the owner acting on the same aged request, a commit installing a leaf with a new signature key, and a member that misses a rotating commit for good (silent orphan). Properties: **convergence** (T41 among members that held the same candidates and blobs at settle; no settled member ever leaves a branch because of a late or ground commit); **detection** (a member whose branch is dead reaches `resync_needed` within the CO-11 bound once the other branch's traffic reaches it; a subset cut off for good raises `partition_suspected` within 9 days, and the majority raises `split_suspected` within the retention plus 72 h when the subset posts; a silent orphan raises `stale_suspected` within 9 days); **no stranded committer** (a committer whose own commit others merged ends on their branch, by merging it or by a rejoin, whatever its rank); **no invalid merge** (no rank-255 commit with proposals or above the CO-14 rate, no removal of a lower rank, no Add without valid evidence, no swap without rejoin or restore evidence); **liveness** (with rank 0 online and no partition, every pending request is committed within two passes, and a flooding member never blocks a committer's pass); **catch-up latency** (a member back after N ≤ 30 epochs whose commits are still on the relays is current after one listing pass of one session, with no wall-clock wait); **Welcomes** (no joiner opens a Welcome of a commit that did not win); **removal hold** (no post encrypted in epoch e by an honest member after it held the removing candidate for e); **rotation** (a removal always yields a generation the removed member cannot name). Mutants MM-2, MM-3, MM-4, MM-16, MM-19, MM-21, MM-23, MM-24, MM-26, MM-27, MM-32, MM-33, MM-34 and MM-36 must fail EM-3. Nightly job `channel-state-models`.

### 12.4 Crash enumeration (CS-C1, CS-C2; the F7 §8.3 method)

| Scenario | Content |
|---|---|
| **SCN-CA** create and admit | create; invite; accept; join request; the committer's pass; Welcome; join |
| **SCN-CB** posts | 3 members, 30 posts with SHUFFLE and REPEATS across 3 relays, a burst of 40 from one sender (peek ordering), a RESURRECTed post, a COPY_NAMESPACE'd post |
| **SCN-CC** membership | an add and a remove in one pass, settle, rotation, the new generation's funding and registrations, drain |
| **SCN-CD** concurrency | ranks 0 and 1 commit on one epoch, **the losing one with an Add**; a rank-255 self-update on the same epoch; the loser clears, deletes its Welcome and re-passes; the joiner receives exactly one Welcome, of the winning commit, and its KeyPackage is consumed once (MM-19); **INDETERMINATE variant** (§19.13): the committer's store closes INDETERMINATE while the relays did keep a copy; the others merge it and post; the committer merges its own commit from their successors and never strands (MM-32) |
| **SCN-CE** offline | a member offline across 5 epochs and one rotation, then catch-up in order; **latency assertion**: current after one listing pass of its first session, with no wall-clock wait (CO-4 anchors, `reofferDeferred`); a variant across 30 epochs with the same assertion; a variant with EXPIRE_LOG → `resync_needed` → rejoin |
| **SCN-CF** ephemeral | 5 min, 1 h and 7 d channels; displayed, unread, pending; a timer change; a clock step back |
| **SCN-CG** fork | FORK mode splits members; both sides settle; a one-member side finds its branch dead (CO-11) and rejoins; a several-member side raises `split_suspected` and rejoins after the scripted fingerprint comparison; the owner's side unaffected |
| **SCN-CH** faults | `SQLITE_FULL` at every statement of RECV, PASS, SETTLE; `SimulatedNativeCrash` in `receive` and `settle` (poison rules of F9 §7.8) |
| **SCN-CI** migration | a v5 database with live DM and sync rows → v6 → v7, then SCN-CA |
| **SCN-CJ** reveal | a reveal to one member; others open nothing; the recipient adds the contact (source `link`, trust `unverified`); after the recipient's next leaf refresh and the old epoch's retention, its seed and state open no earlier REVEAL (PC-18) |
| **SCN-CK** leave and evict | a leave, an eviction at 63 days, a restore swap (it rotates; the replaced stack is a T38 subject) and a rejoin swap (it does not rotate); a moderator's resignation and leave; the owner's transfer of rank 0 and its leave (§19.13) |
| **SCN-CL** withheld removal (§19.1, PRV-H4) | WITHHOLD_COMMIT hides a removing commit **for good** from a subset S of two active members; S keeps posting; assertions: the majority raises `split_suspected` within the retention of S's epoch plus 72 h while the old namespace drains, S raises `partition_suspected` within 9 days, the removed member's reads of S's posts are counted and reported (the declared L8-C17 bound, not a zero), and S's `resync` after the fingerprint comparison restores it; **silent-orphan variant** (§19.15, SILENT_ORPHAN): one member misses a rotating commit for good; it raises `stale_suspected` within 9 days (MM-36) and its `resync` inside the drain restores it, after the drain it reaches `reinvite_needed` |
| **SCN-CM** adversarial members (§19.1, §19.3, §19.5) | LATE_COMMIT with a better rank after everyone settled (nobody moves; a returning member picks the branch with successors); GRIND by a member keeping an old state (nobody moves; catch-up picks the successors' branch); FLOOD_SELFUPDATE (CO-14 refuses the excess, committers still pass and remove the flooder); SPAM_UNOPENABLE in a quiet channel (a member may enter `resync_needed` and returns to `active` at the next progress; its rejoin is refused); REPLAY_JOIN (an old join KeyPackage as rejoin or restore is refused, a copied rejoin completes the requester's own rejoin, the victim is never evicted); a member whose system clock is 10 days slow (`clock_blocked`, no divergence, resumes when the clock is fixed); **added by §19.14 … §19.18:** SPAM_UNOPENABLE by a member removed at the end of the old generation in the draining namespace (nobody enters `resync_needed`, MM-37); RESEAL_ORDER (the victim's posts all display, MM-35); PROPOSE (dropped and counted; posting continues, MM-30); FORGE_OWN (dropped and counted; the victim's own-commit bookkeeping is unaffected); NEW_SIGNER (the commit is invalid everywhere, MM-31); REPLACE_BOOTSTRAP (the joiner refuses the substituted bootstrap and keeps waiting for a valid Welcome, PC-17) |

After every reboot: `integrity_check`, `foreign_key_check`, every trigger present; at most one current and one draining generation per channel; ≤ 8 candidates per epoch plus the own commit; no Welcome op for a commit that is not the merged winner; `mls_kv` rows open under the state key; the reload oracle; every `queued` post has an op or a pending row; epoch keys exist for exactly the retained epochs. At quiescence: CS-C2, CS-C3, CS-C6, T40 (every store maps to a non-inbound cause), liveness (one more post decrypts everywhere).

### 12.5 The exit scenario SCN-M20 ("20 clienți add/remove/resync")

**Setup:** 20 clients; 3 relays over 2 operators (one per scenario variant hostile); privacy mode STANDARD, and HIGH once the S1 harness honours send delays (§19.10; until then the report says "STANDARD only"); channel timer 7 d (a 1 h variant for the T25 clause); rank 0 = client 1, rank 1 = client 2; funders = clients 1 and 2.

**Script (virtual time ≈ 80 days, anchored so that its last day is the host day, §12.1, so that the 63-day eviction and a 30-day offline window fit):** client 1 creates the channel and invites 19 clients over three days (invites by link; batches bounded by the floor); ≈ 200 posts with reactions and replies; **removals:** a moderator removal, a leave, an idle eviction (a client silent 63 days — time is virtual), and a **device swap** (client 7 "restores": a new stack with the same seed rejoins through a new invite; the restore rotates the generation; its old stack keeps its state and is a seizure subject), with an **owner-restore variant** in which client 1 (rank 0) restores the same way and keeps rank 0 (§19.13, MM-33); two clients offline across ≥ 5 epochs and one rotation, then **resync** from the relays; one client offline past EXPIRE_LOG, which reaches `resync_needed` and rejoins; ranks 0 and 1 commit concurrently on one epoch (CO-3); a **FORK** variant in which the model relays serve two commits to two subsets for longer than the settle window: a subset of one member finds its branch dead (CO-11) and rejoins; a subset of several active members raises `split_suspected`, and its scripted users, whose fingerprint differs from the owner's, run `resync`; a removed client re-invited, returning with the same pseudonym (D12).

**Assertions:** at every epoch, every honest current member's `(winner_hash, epoch_authenticator)` is equal (T41); no duplicate display, one order everywhere; **the removed-member oracle opens 0** blobs of later epochs for each removed client and for the old stack of client 7, and 0 blobs of any later generation for that old stack (the plan's second clause, LIMITE:39; §19.16), and 0 posts composed after their author first held the removing commit (CO-13); the joiner oracle opens 0 for each joiner; T9 and T13 on every blob and post; the channel T25 cases pass (the plan's third clause); T37 and T40 on the world's relay view.

**Scale:** every PR runs SCN-M20 with one seed and crash singles on SCN-CA … SCN-CD; `channel-exit-gate` runs it with seeds pinned after S0 (target: 64, the number Phase 9's gate actually ran) and the full crash enumeration.

### 12.6 Functional suites

| Suite | Cases |
|---|---|
| **RO-C reorder** | all orders of 6 posts of one sender; bursts of 40 over 3 relays with HIGH delays; posts after the next commit (REORDER_ACROSS_EPOCH) within and beyond retention; **re-seal** (RESEAL_ORDER: a member re-seals a victim's late post under an early `order`; the copy is dropped at `open`, every post of the victim displays, MM-35); a burst of 40 with 20 withheld past the hold (the declared ratchet-range loss is counted, §6.3.6); a committer posting while its own non-removing commit is pending (the posts decrypt everywhere as late posts of e, §19.13) |
| **RS-C restart** | process death at every lane item and actor step; a member offline 60 days in a 30-day channel |
| **RP-C replay** | each dedup layer isolated (§6.3.4); a resurrected commit; a copied join request; a member's proposal (dropped unprocessed; the receiver's next post is not refused, MM-30); a forged "own" message (dropped, counted) |
| **JN join** | every admission refusal of §6.2.4 (A) and (B); single use across two committers present since the invite; expiry; **an invite of a removed sponsor refused** (void at settle, and CO-2 refuses the Add if a committer admits it anyway); a committer that joined after the `INVITE_ISSUED` does not admit it; a joiner admitted after the `INVITE_ISSUED` accepts a later Add of that invite (evidence in the AAD); a join during a rotation (Welcome in the draining namespace); a rejoin by a current pseudonym with each freshness refusal of §6.7; a restore by invite for a current pseudonym (a swap); an old join KeyPackage re-wrapped as rejoin or restore refused; `GhostKpBind` of another channel or purpose refused; an invite for a channel already joined; **added by §19.16:** a WELCOME whose bootstrap a holder of `JK_g` replaced (refused, PC-17); a bootstrap naming the invite's generation with a different namespace (refused); a rejoin and a restore Welcome with the old group's rows present (the rows are not passed; no `GroupAlreadyExists`); a restore of the owner and of a moderator by a lower-ranked committer (admitted, rank kept) |
| **GV governance** | CO-2 rules (removal of a lower rank refused, context change by rank 1 refused, rotation flag from rank 255 refused, a swap without evidence refused, a rank-255 commit above the CO-14 rate refused, an update-path leaf with a wrong `refresh_epoch` refused; **added by §19.13, §19.14:** an update-path leaf with a new signature key, or with a credential identity other than its key, refused (MM-31); a resignation accepted, a resignation by the last committer refused, a transfer of rank 0 by rank 0 accepted and by rank 1 refused; a swap of rank 0 by rank 1 with rejoin or restore evidence accepted; a moderator's scheduled pass ignoring work younger than 48 h, and its user-action Remove committed at once (MM-34); rank read from the pre-commit context for a commit that reorders `committers`); moderator list changes; the S5 simulation over the threshold function |
| **FD funding** | renewal at the PRF time; rotation funding by the rotating committer at its switch hour; a non-rotating funder's first redemption for the new generation at its next EXPIRING renewal, not at the settle (PC-6); a funder without tokens (stores wait, then complete); registrations lapsing after the drain; periodic and invite-expiry rotations (Q28), never while a generation drains; a restore rotates, a rejoin does not (MM-18, MM-29); the drain's end with deferred junk rows still fetched (marked consumed, then `Namespaces.remove` succeeds, §6.5) |
| **FK fork** | FORK at a rotation (two generation secrets): recovery through the old generation during the drain; a late candidate for a settled epoch changes nothing but a fork signal; forged fork signals by a member removed at e + 1 change nothing |
| **EP-C ephemeral** | §8.5 |
| **RV reveal** | recipient-only opening; binding checks; a forged reveal (wrong pseudonym) refused; a reveal addressed to a box key the recipient has since replaced still opens within the old epoch's retention and not after (PC-18); the contact is added with source `link`; the `ch_reveal` rows disappear with the REVEAL post's timer |

### 12.7 The channel privacy world

A world of 60 clients, 12 channels (5–20 members; at most 9 per client), invites, removals and rotations, DMs present, modes STANDARD and HIGH (HIGH, JC-6's send-delay rules, NI-CH3 and the registration half of JC-3 need the S1 harness extensions of §19.10: the redeem lane inside the world, send delays, circuit timing; until they exist each report line names what it compared); PR scale 28 virtual days, one seed; gate scale 56 days, seeds pinned after S0. Exact searches over the effective relay view (every capture event, stored bytes, `keys.redb`, circuits):

| ID | Search | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| JC-1 | every relay-visible value recomputed from public material (channel ids known to members, pseudonym and box keys, KeyPackages, invite ids) | 0 matches |
| JC-2 | stored blob bytes scanned for group ids, epochs, KeyPackageRefs, pseudonym keys, `ChannelContent` headers | 0 |
| JC-3 | distinct store scopes and read scopes per (relay, generation namespace) | exactly 1 and 1; registration capabilities only in `register` |
| JC-4 | per (generation namespace, timer version) in 7-day, 30-day and off channels: distinct TTLs, and buckets of commits vs posts ≤ the floor (revised §19.22: a timer change between those values moves the TTL inside one namespace; join requests under an invite from an earlier timer version are counted and reported as the declared exception) | 1 TTL per timer version; commits only in the floor bucket |
| JC-5 | a removed member's credentials or secrets accepted in a later generation's namespace | 0 |
| JC-6 | the relay-visible time of any store equals, within the send-delay rules, the time of a non-inbound cause in `oracle_traffic` | all |
| JC-7 (added §19.20) | the model relay tries every value it was shown — every READ and DEPOSIT secret inside a presented credential, every verifier, every registration capability, and the namespace — as the key input K and as the recognition key R of §5.6 (every mode, every bucket) on every blob it stores | 0 hints match, 0 blobs open |
| NI-CH1 | twins differing only in what one client received (valid post, forged blob, replay, a join request addressed to it as committer, a commit, a rejoin request) | identical views of that client, except LC-6's pass outcome |
| NI-CH2 | twins where a committer stores a non-removing commit vs a post of equal length at the same minute | identical views (revised §19.22: since a non-removing own commit no longer holds the committer's posts, §19.13, the views stay identical after the store too; a **removing** commit is compared only up to its store, because the removal hold then pauses every member's posts, the committer's first, CO-13) |
| NI-CH3 | twins where a funder funds 1 vs 4 channels at fixed renewal times | identical per-namespace views; the count of funded namespaces is declared (LC-3) |
| NI-CH4 | twins where a client receives a rotating commit vs a non-rotating commit of equal length at the same minute (§19.8) | identical views except LC-4's declared difference: the first list of `ns_{g+1}` at the commit's switch hour (or the next session start), no redemption unless the client is the rotating committer or at its next renewal as a funder, the removed member's listing unchanged until the drain ends |

### 12.8 Seizure, removal and joiner oracles

- **Removed-member oracle (T38):** dump the removed client's database, WAL, `mls_kv` and native context; try every stored 32-byte value, every retained `K_ep` and `JK_g`, every past-epoch secret (through `MlsGroup::load` of its stale state and `process_message`) and every KeyPackage key against every mode-0x11 blob of every epoch after its removal and every blob of every later generation (the join traffic of the draining generation, which it may open by design, L8-C5, excepted). Opened must be 0. Variants: removed while offline; removed while its own commit was pending; re-added (the epochs between removal and re-add open 0); **hold** (§19.6): every post composed by an honest member after its first sighting of the removing candidate opens 0 under the removed member's keys, and the posts it can open (composed before their author's sighting) are counted and reported against the L8-C18 bound.
- **Joiner oracle (T39):** the same over all blobs of epochs before the join epoch; the generation's earlier join traffic, which the joiner opens by design with the invite's `JK_g`, is counted and reported, not asserted to be 0 (§19.22).
- **Restore variant (T38, §19.16):** the replaced stack's dump against every blob of every generation after the restoring commit: 0.
- **Device seizure (AD-8, declared bounds; corrected §19.19):** a current member's dump opens exactly the current epoch's unconsumed messages from the epoch's start and the retained epochs' unconsumed messages (§8.3), measured against every recorded blob of the world (not only those still on relays), and the report prints the age of the oldest message opened against the L8-C10 bound; no expired post's plaintext remains (T25 physical scan).

### 12.9 Mutants (registered only with their killer; counts pinned after S0)

| ID | Mutation | Killer |
|---|---|---|
| MM-1 | change-set applied before OK (partial state) | SCN-CH, reload oracle |
| MM-2 | own commit merged without a verified copy | EM-3, SCN-CD |
| MM-3 | no settle window | EM-3, SCN-M20 FORK |
| MM-4 | tie-break by hash only | EM-3 |
| MM-5 | rank-255 commit with proposals accepted | GV, EM-3 |
| MM-6 | context change from rank ≥ 1 accepted | GV |
| MM-7 | no rotation on removal | T38 oracle, JC-5 |
| MM-8 | next generation derived from the pre-commit epoch | T38 oracle |
| MM-9 | past epochs kept beyond retention | seizure bound test |
| MM-10 | posts encrypted at compose while not current | RO-C (late posts lost beyond retention) |
| MM-11 | hash-order decryption (no peek ordering) | SCN-CB burst |
| MM-12 | unopenable blobs dropped instead of deferred | SCN-CE |
| MM-13 | invite single-use not enforced | JN |
| MM-14 | `invite_sig` not verified | JN (sock-puppet case) |
| MM-15 | KeyPackage checks skipped (extra leaf extension, oversized) | JN |
| MM-16 | finality not sticky: a settled member re-decides on a later (late or ground) commit | SCN-CM (LATE_COMMIT, GRIND), EM-3 |
| MM-17 | eviction ignores self-updates | CK |
| MM-18 | rejoin rotates the generation | FD, SCN-CK |
| MM-19 | Welcomes enqueued at the pass, not at the settle of a won commit | SCN-CD (losing commit with an Add), EM-3 |
| MM-20 | rejoin admitted without freshness (seen KeyPackage, stale day, live leaf, claimed epoch on the committer's branch) | JN, SCN-CM (REPLAY_JOIN) |
| MM-21 | CO-2 accepts a swap without rejoin or restore evidence | GV, EM-3 |
| MM-22 | an invite of a removed sponsor admitted (no void, no CO-2 sponsor check) | JN |
| MM-23 | CO-2 reads local history (an Add checked against the member's own `INVITE_ISSUED` records) | JN (a later joiner diverges; `oracle_epoch`), EM-3 |
| MM-24 | no successor preference at settle (plain `(rank, hash)`) | SCN-CM (catch-up after LATE_COMMIT), EM-3 |
| MM-25 | settle anchored at the local first sighting only (no store-hour anchor, no re-offer) | SCN-CE latency assertion |
| MM-26 | no CO-14 rate, or a committer's pass blocked by a rank-255 candidate | SCN-CM (FLOOD_SELFUPDATE), EM-3 |
| MM-27 | a lifetime failure treated as an invalid commit (the member picks another candidate) | SCN-CM (slow clock), EM-3 |
| MM-28 | past epochs deleted by `before_timestamp(day)` instead of by count | RO-C late post within retention; seizure bound test |
| MM-29 | a restore swap does not rotate the generation (§19.16) | T38 restore variant (the replaced stack opens a later generation's blobs), FD |
| MM-30 | a received proposal is stored (`store_pending_proposal`) instead of dropped (§19.16) | RP-C proposal case (the member's next post is refused `PendingProposal`) |
| MM-31 | CO-2 accepts an installed leaf whose signature key changed or whose credential identity differs from its key (§19.14) | GV (commits built with a changed signer and with a mismatched credential in the test-only builder) |
| MM-32 | the committer clears its own commit when the op closes INDETERMINATE (round 10b's rule, §19.13) | SCN-CD INDETERMINATE variant (the others merge the commit; the committer must merge it from its successors, not strand) |
| MM-33 | CO-2 refuses a swap of a committer by a higher rank (§19.13) | SCN-M20 owner-restore variant; SCN-CG with the owner on the dead side |
| MM-34 | a moderator's scheduled pass includes work younger than `r × 48 h` (§19.13) | GV (a fresh join request is not committed by rank 1 while rank 0 is active); EM-3 concurrency count |
| MM-35 | peek order taken from the seal's `order` without the `ChannelOrder` check (§19.18) | RO-C re-seal case (a member re-seals a victim's post with a false `order`; no post of the victim is lost) |
| MM-36 | no `stale_suspected` (§19.15) | SCN-CL silent-orphan variant (a member misses a rotating commit past EXPIRE_LOG; the flag rises within 9 days), EM-3 detection |
| MM-37 | CO-11 counts unopenable blobs in a draining namespace (§19.15) | SCN-CM spam from a member removed at the end of the old generation (no member enters `resync_needed`) |
| PC-1 | raw MLS stored (no seal) | JC-2 |
| PC-2 | one seal key per channel instead of per epoch | joiner oracle, T38 oracle |
| PC-3 | bucket by type | JC-4, NI-CH2 |
| PC-4 | members store with token-bought capabilities | JC-3 |
| PC-5 | a committer's pass triggered by a received request | NI-CH1, JC-6 |
| PC-6 | a non-rotating funder creates registration rows (and so redeems) at the settle or switch of a received rotation instead of at its next renewal | NI-CH1, NI-CH4, FD |
| PC-7 | namespace derived from `channel_id` | JC-1 |
| PC-8 | commit TTL ≠ post TTL in a 7-day channel | JC-4 |
| PC-9 | reveal names its recipient in content | RV |
| PC-10 | registration capability used for list or store | JC-3 |
| PC-11 | bootstrap readable under `JK_g` | T38 oracle (join-traffic variant) |
| PC-12 | second-level time in content or lifetimes | T13, T33 scan |
| PC-13 | `order` outside the AEAD | JC-2 |
| PC-14 | no removal hold (posts encrypted in e while a removing candidate is held) | T38 hold variant |
| PC-15 | namespace switch at the settle (fetch-time) instead of the switch hour, or the removed member stops listening at its merge | NI-CH4 |
| PC-16 | a seal, recognition or join key derived from a value the relays are shown (a READ or DEPOSIT secret, a verifier, a credential) (§19.20) | JC-7 |
| PC-17 | the bootstrap fallback without authentication (HPKE base mode to `box_pk`, or under `JK_g`) (§19.16) | JN re-sealed-bootstrap case (a holder of `JK_g` substitutes a bootstrap; the joiner refuses it); T38 join-traffic variant |
| PC-18 | box keys derived from the pseudonym seed instead of drawn per leaf (§19.17) | RV forward-secrecy case (after a leaf refresh and the old epoch's retention, M's seed and state open no earlier REVEAL) |
| ME-C1 | reactions not cascaded | EP-C |
| ME-C2 | unread deadline from content, not relay expiry | EP-C |
| ME-C3 | no checkpoint after a cascade | T25 physical scan |
| ME-C4 | `added_at` day rewrite skipped | T33 state scan |
| ME-C5 | a row derived from a post outlives its rule of §8.5 (a `ch_reveal` row after its post's timer, an invite record after its expiry) (§19.21) | EP-C, T25 physical scan |
| ME-C6 | `ch_epoch.anchor_minute` kept after the epoch is final (§19.21) | the schema trigger test; T33 introspection |

### 12.10 Fuzzing

`mls-fuzz` (nightly, dated pinned nightly toolchain, `client-core/mls/fuzz` outside the workspace with its own lock checked by cargo-deny): **new work** — the `dm-fuzz` arrangement F9 planned was never built (STATUS, Phase 9 row: "vendor-tests și fuzzing-ul neconstruite"), so S11 builds the arrangement once, generic over the DM and MLS crates (§19.10, F-4). Targets `open` (every mode), `contentDecode`, `inviteParse`, `joinVerify`, `receive` on arbitrary MLS bytes after a valid seal (the pre-authentication decoders of GHSA-rrmv and GHSA-w62v, reachable only by seal-key holders), `welcomeJoin`. OpenMLS's own decode targets run at the pinned tag in `mls-vendor-tests`.

### 12.11 Emulator and devices

`dm-emulator.yml` gains a channel smoke test: the release variant loads `libghost_client_net.so`, creates a channel, admits a second in-process client, exchanges posts, removes it; the host/Android equivalence test (F9 §15.9) gains deterministic MLS cases. **It has never run on the runner (no KVM; STATUS:18)**; the local Windows emulator run of F9 §22.32 is the fallback. A device checklist step (2 devices, one channel, add, post, remove, rejoin) joins F9 §15.10 and stays manual. **arm64 (added §19.23):** OpenMLS and the libcrux provider have never run on arm64 in GHOST — upstream builds Android targets without testing them (`om:README.md:34-47`), and the emulator image is x86_64 — so the device step on **two arm64 phones** is a named owner action before any distribution (§15 item 13, Q20); it is not a Phase 10 gate item, and no arm64 claim is made until it runs.

### 12.12 The exit-gate argument, and what it does not prove

| Claim | JVM (every PR and nightly) | Emulator | Devices | Staging / external |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| add, remove, resync with 20 clients | SCN-M20, EM-3, crash enumeration | smoke | checklist (2 devices) | — |
| removed member decrypts no new epoch | T38 oracle | — | — | — |
| T25 in channels | EP-C, physical scans | T25 smoke | checklist | — |
| relay blindness (T36, T37, T40) | privacy world | — | — | **staging capture not run** (the F9 O1 runbook) |
| real SQLCipher, Keystore, Tor | — | partly (loopback relays) | checklist | — |
| performance on low-end phones | host timings only (a 15–25× factor, estimated) | — | **not measured** | — |
| the composition's security | tests, EM-3 | — | — | **external review: not done; a release gate** |
