### 13.1 Gates (each with a negative fixture and a `self-test.sh` hit count)

| Gate | Rule | Supports |
|---|---|---|
| **G-M1** `rust-client-allowlist.sh`, `rust-dependency-allowlist.txt`, `rust-crypto-pins.txt` | the new crate names of the OpenMLS graph, generated in S0 by `cargo tree -e normal` on both shipped triples (the research probe's list: `openmls`, `openmls_traits`, `openmls_libcrux_crypto`, `openmls_memory_storage`, `openmls_serialization_helpers`, `hpke-rs`, `hpke-rs-crypto`, `hpke-rs-libcrux`, `libcrux-aead`, `libcrux-aes`, `libcrux-chacha20poly1305`, `libcrux-poly1305`, `libcrux-ed25519`, `libcrux-hkdf`, `libcrux-hmac`, `libcrux-hmac-drbg`, `tls_codec`, `tls_codec_derive`, `rayon`, `rayon-core`, `crossbeam-deque`, `crossbeam-epoch`, `crossbeam-utils`, `either`, … — minus names already present); exact pins of `openmls`, `openmls_traits`, `openmls_libcrux_crypto`, `hpke-rs*`; `cargo deny check` green | T7 |
| **G-M2** `rust-feature-policy.sh` | the forbidden OpenMLS features of §3.2 absent from every shipped graph; `test-utils` allowed only in the test-only crate `ghost-client-mls-kat` and the `mls-vendor-tests` checkout (§12.2), neither of which any shipped or host library depends on; no GHOST code names `openmls_memory_storage` or `openmls_basic_credential`, or calls `CommitBuilder::build_with_new_signer` or `MlsGroup::store_pending_proposal` (§19.14, §19.16) | ADR-40 |
| **G-M3** `ch-derivation-inputs.sh` + a Rust test | every `derive_*` of `ghost-client-mls` takes a typed secret input (`EpochExport`, `GenerationSecret`, `PseudonymSeed`, `InviteSeed`) | T36 static half |
| **G-M4** `mls-rust-no-logging.sh` | no logger installed in any graph; `log` reached only as a dependency; no `println!`, `eprintln!`, `dbg!` in GHOST sources | T3 |
| **G-M5** `ch-enqueue-callers.sh` | `Outbox.enqueue`, `Registrations.*`, `Funding.set`, `Namespaces.register/remove` and `Capabilities.put` for channel namespaces are called only from `CH-CREATE`, `CH-INVITE`, `CH-ACCEPT`, `CH-POST`, `CH-PASS`, `CH-SETTLE` (`Outbox.enqueue` of its own winning commit's Welcomes only), `CH-SWITCH` (namespaces, credentials, the rotating committer's `Funding.set`), `CH-REJOIN`, `CH-REVEAL`, `CH-LEAVE`, the funder renewal step and `CH-SWEEP` (removals) — never from `CH-RECV` or `CH-JOIN` (a joiner's newer generation is switched by `CH-SWITCH` at its next session start, §19.8) | T40 static half |
| **G-M6** `mls-jni-surface.sh` (G-R6 extended) | the exported `Java_org_ghost_crypto_bridge_MlsNative_*` symbols of the shipped library equal §10.2's list; the host library's equal the list plus its test entry | ADR-19, ADR-40 |
| **G-M7** `mls-golden-lock.sh` | at an OpenMLS pin change: golden channel states and the upgrade test for the old and new pins, the golden `MessageSecrets` (day rewrite), the exhaustive error `match`, the WG vector replay through `GhostKv` | ADR-40 |
| **G-M8** `mls-vendored-clock-log.sh` (the G-R8 method) | clock, logging and network sites in the compiled code of the `ghost-client-mls` graph equal a committed, justified baseline (the OpenMLS clock sites of §3.6 among them) | T3, T33 |
| **G-M9** portable libcrux (G-R3's rule) extended | the provider's libcrux crates built portable-only on both triples (V10-7). **There is no `libcrux-portable.sh`**: G-R3's rule is inline in `dm-emulator.yml:30-31,176-189` ("inline until `libcrux-portable.sh` exists", F9 §22.9 item 15); S0 decides between extending the inline rule and extracting the script for both graphs (§19.10). **`libcrux-aes` joins the rule and every clean list (§19.23):** the provider's `libcrux-aead` 0.0.9 is used with default features, which include `aesgcm128` and `aesgcm256` and so compile `libcrux-aes` 0.0.9, whose `build.rs` reads `LIBCRUX_DISABLE_SIMD128/256` and `LIBCRUX_ENABLE_SIMD128/256` without any `rerun-if-env-changed` line — the stale-cfg hazard F9 measured in V-6 for `libcrux-ml-kem`, `-sha3` and `-intrinsics`; the `cargo clean -p …` lines of `ci.yml:254`, `dm-emulator.yml:149,165,172` and `dm-privacy-gate.yml:114,197` gain `-p libcrux-aes` | ADR-36 practice |
| G-L1 … G-L4 | notices regenerated with the OpenMLS graph; the three `hpke-rs` crates' sources in the release bundle (G-L4) | ADR-37 |
| `apk-native-libs.sh` | still one native library | T12 |
| `anti-placeholder.sh`, `no-logging.sh`, `sync-no-catch-all.sh`, `kotlin-clearnet.sh`, G-DM1, G-DM4, G-DM6 | scopes cover `client-core/mls` and the channel packages | T3, T6, T8 |

**Phase 9 gates this design leans on that are not built yet** (STATUS:18): G-R3, G-R4's graph half, G-R7, G-R8. G-M4, G-M7, G-M8 and G-M9 are their MLS counterparts; S0 decides whether each pair is built once, generically, for both graphs (recommended).

**Runner budget (Q20; revised §19.11).** The four runners live on the owner's laptop and run only while he is logged in (`docs/ci-self-hosted.md` §1, §3). The nightly load already scheduled, by job timeout: `dm-emulator` 150 min, `dm-privacy-gate` 300 + 90, `dm-state-models` 60, `entitlement-exit-gate` 12 shards × 120, `messaging-exit-gate` 480, `monero-regtest` 45, `signer-timing` 60, `t2-exit-gate` a 10-pair matrix × 240 (+120), whose pair 0 has already hit its timeout four times (reported by the feasibility review from the run history; not re-checked here) (`.github/workflows/*.yml`, `cron` and `timeout-minutes`; `pq-model` and `pq-bump` run on push, not nightly). Phase 10 adds three nightly gates (one of them a 60-client, 56-day world with real OpenMLS), nightly fuzzing and weekly vendor tests. **S0 measures the total nightly occupancy** (every scheduled job, not only the PR `android` job) and sets shards and a rotation of gates across nights so that a night fits the hours the laptop is on; `channel-exit-gate` and `channel-privacy-gate` alternate nights unless the measurement shows room. The laptop's availability is a declared dependency of every nightly claim (R-7).

**Effort, stated in two parts (revised §19.11).**

*Implementation.* F9's observed pace is the only calibration there is: the code of its slices landed in about seven active days (2026-09-16 and 2026-09-19 … 09-24, `git log`) against an estimate of 10–12 weeks, while about two thirds of its registered mutants (30 of 97 run), its fuzzing, its vendor tests, its seeded randomness and its HIGH mode stayed unbuilt. That pace is therefore a lower bound for writing code and says nothing about closing a gate. At that pace this phase's slices sum to **about 34.5 working days** (the column above; round 10c added about 2.5 days in S4, S6, S7 and S9 for the corrections of §19.13 … §19.22; S5 and the S1 harness debts carry most of the uncertainty), roughly 7 weeks — inside the plan's 6–9 weeks for implementation only. **Not included:** the channel-media slice (§5.10), which needs Phase 11's engine and is sized when Q38 is answered.

*Closure blockers (calendar, not effort).* (1) **CI restored** — the artifact quota and a KVM runner or the local emulator fallback; owner (Q20); needed by the S10 and S11 exits, not before. (2) **Nightly capacity** — V10-14 measures it; the channel gates alternate nights with the existing load, so a green gate at the pinned seed count takes several nights; S0 owns the plan, the owner's laptop hours bound it (R-7). (3) **Seven fuzz nights** after S11's arrangement lands (§15 item 8). (4) **Owner decisions** Q1–Q39 before S0's freeze, among them the ADRs this phase builds on (ADR-25, ADR-30′ … ADR-39; Q39). (5) **Phase 9's open gate** (R-8): defects found under channels are fixed in Phase 9 code; whether Phase 10 may close first is Q39. (5a) **Two arm64 phones** for the device step, before distribution (§12.11). (6) The external review stays a release gate, outside this phase. The calendar to the exit gate is therefore implementation plus at least two weeks of nightly runs once (1) is done, and it is not shortened by Q3, Q15 or Q20: Q3 saves about one working day, Q15's default is already assumed in the column, and Q20 changes CI time, not effort. Phase 9's record says a design's test counts slip; §12 pins none before S0.

## 15. Exit-gate checklist (all on the closing commit)

1. SCN-M20 green in `channel-exit-gate` with the pinned seeds and the full crash enumeration (plan clause 1, FR-2.2, FR-3.2, FR-3.5).
2. The removed-member oracle opens 0 in every variant, including the device-swap stack (later epochs and later generations: a restore rotates) and the removal-hold variant (plan clause 2, TM S4, LIMITE:39); the posts it opens under the declared L8-C18 residue are reported, not hidden.
3. The channel T25 cases green, including the physical scans (plan clause 3).
4. EM-3 green with the §19.1 and §19.13 adversaries; T41 in SCN-M20 and its owner-restore variant; SCN-CL (with the silent-orphan variant) and SCN-CM green with their declared bounds.
5. The privacy world at gate scale: JC-1 … JC-7 as expected, NI-CH1 … NI-CH4 equal up to their declared differences (T36, T37, T40), on the harness of §19.10.
6. Every registered mutant detected; `ch-report-check.sh` accepts the reports.
7. The WG test vectors pass in `mls-vendor-tests` (the openmls checkout at `3a3e35de…`); the passive-client replay through `GhostKv` passes on the V10-12 subset, and the report names the vectors and epochs left out for expired KeyPackages (spec Appendix A; §12.2).
8. Fuzzing ran nightly for at least 7 nights with no open crash.
9. Gates G-M1 … G-M9 green with fixtures; `cargo deny` green; notices and MPL sources updated.
10. The emulator smoke test ran (on the runner, or the local fallback recorded); the device step recorded or recorded as not executed.
10a. The harness features the gate lines rely on (§19.10: seeded native randomness or record/replay, the redeem lane, send delays and HIGH mode, circuit timing) are built, or each report line names what it did not compare.
11. Appendix B applied to INVARIANTS, THREAT_MODEL, LIMITE, the plan, STATUS and the ADR index; ADR-40 … ADR-43 filed as "propus".
12. The phase's adversarial review done and its findings corrected or recorded.
12a. **FR-3.4's media half** ("media up to 50 MiB", "boundary-size, invalid-media … tests") is either met by the channel-media slice or recorded as not met, according to Q38; the closing report does not claim FR-3.4 in full otherwise.
13. **Not required for the gate, required before distribution:** the external audit of OpenMLS as configured and of the GHOST composition (plan row 15), staging captures (T1), device performance, and the device step on two arm64 phones (§12.11; OpenMLS and libcrux have never run on arm64 in GHOST).

## 16. Risks

| # | Risk | Likelihood / impact | Mitigation | Residue |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | Commit arbitration has a subtle bug (divergence, lost admissions, liveness); the review rounds found such defects in round 10a (§19.1, §19.2, §19.5) and more in round 10b's text (a stranded committer, an owner that could neither leave nor be restored, moderators racing the owner, §19.13) | medium / high | EM-3 exhaustive model with adversarial members and relays, FORK, WITHHOLD_COMMIT, LATE_COMMIT, GRIND, FLOOD and the §19.13 cases, reload oracle, `oracle_epoch` at every epoch, MM-2 … MM-4, MM-16, MM-19 … MM-28, MM-31 … MM-34 | forks under AD-1 partitions remain possible by design: detected when the branches' traffic meets, bounded and flagged when it does not (L8-C17, L8-C21) |
| R-2 | OpenMLS's serde layout changes at a bump (the day rewrite, the storage format) | medium / medium | exact pins; G-M7 golden states and upgrade tests; `STATE_CORRUPT` instead of silent defaults | a bump costs a migration drill |
| R-3 | Value semantics cost on low-end phones: every call reloads the channel's rows (≈ 57–63 KB at 20 members and ≈ 225–261 KB at 100 in the probe's memory storage with 2 past epochs; GHOST keeps fewer) | medium / medium | V10-5, V10-10; retention short (§8.3); a version-checked native cache as Q18's alternative | unmeasured on devices until S0 or Phase 12 |
| R-4 | APK size: +2.7–3.3 MB per ABI (probe) | high / low | S0 measures in the shipped library; budget ≤ +3 MiB per ABI | NFR-4 (< 80 MiB) not at risk by itself |
| R-5 | Token budget and funding stalls (rotations cost 3 tokens; funders absent) | medium / medium | ≥ 2 funders recommended in the Phase 13 UI; registrations tolerate 2–3 weeks; stores wait, never fail silently | L8-C6 |
| R-6 | Pre-authentication decoding bugs in OpenMLS | low / medium | the seal limits who can reach the decoders to seal-key holders; `catch_unwind`; `mls-fuzz`; exact pins with advisory watch | members and invite holders can still reach them |
| R-7 | CI capacity: gates that never run (Phase 9's lesson); four runners on the owner's laptop, only while he is logged in | high / high | S0 measures total nightly occupancy (V10-14); CI restoration required at the S10/S11 exits (Q20); nightly alternation; report checks refuse unmeasured claims | the exit calendar depends on the laptop's hours |
| R-8 | Phase 9's infrastructure is not closed; channel work may expose its defects | high / medium | S0 re-runs the Phase 9 suites the channel engine depends on; defects are fixed in Phase 9's code with their own tests | schedule |
| R-9 | Upstream churn (`ExportedSecret<T>` on `main`; Android "built but not tested" upstream) | medium / low | pins; the emulator equivalence test (x86_64); the device step on two arm64 phones before distribution (§12.11); upstream issues filed (F-5) | arm64 unexercised until the owner runs the device step |
| R-10 | The classical suite: channels exposed to harvest-now-decrypt-later; a later PQ migration needs new channels | certain / medium | declared (L8-C1, §4.5); suite-agile invites; triggers in §4.4 | owner decision Q2 |
| R-11 | Expectations of ADR-13's full governance in Phase 10 | medium / low | D17 and Q15 state the split | — |
| R-12 | GHOST uses OpenMLS in ways its examples do not (no member proposals, by-value adds, `swap_members` for rejoin, the pinned-schema day rewrite) | medium / medium | each is public API except the day rewrite (recorded deviation); golden tests; the external audit scope names them | — |
| R-13 | Loss of the owner's seed, or an owner who vanishes, freezes the policy (committer list) and leaves a leaf nobody can evict (§19.13) | low / medium | resignation and transfer let an owner hand over before leaving; a restored owner keeps rank 0 (any-rank swaps); moderators keep membership moving; the UI (Phase 13) urges naming a moderator | L8-C16; succession of an absent owner is Q37 |
| R-14 | OpenMLS judges KeyPackage lifetimes on the device's system clock, not GHOST's trusted clock (§19.4) | medium / medium | lifetimes `[day − 4, day + 80]`; `clock_blocked` instead of divergence; joins skip the tree-lifetime check; anchored virtual time in worlds; V10-11; an upstream request for an injectable time source (F-5) | a member with a badly wrong system clock stalls until it is fixed (L8-C20) |
| R-15 | The Phase 9 harness debts (seeded randomness, redeem lane, HIGH mode, circuit timing) are larger than sized, or V10-6 fails | medium / medium | sized in S1 and S2 (§14); record/replay as the fallback; report lines name what they compared (§15 item 10a) | privacy and crash claims narrower until built |
| R-16 | The fork rules (successor counts, branch death, split flags) confuse users or are gamed by colluding members | medium / medium | EM-3 adversaries; `split_suspected` with a fingerprint rather than silent moves; Q27 lets the owner prefer the RFC 9750 copy-based option | L8-C21 |
| R-17 | More rotations (invite expiry, 28-day age, restores) raise token use and read pairs | medium / low | at most one non-removal rotation per 14 days and never during a drain; at most two generations listened; 9 channels per client (Q4, Q28) | +3 tokens per rotation for the rotating committer; the read-pair cap can be passed during the six weeks after a slot-table change (L8-C24) |
| R-18 | Channel media (spec FR-3.4, P0) depends on Phase 11's engine, and the two designs had each left it to the other (C-17) | high / medium | §5.10 reserves the contract in Phase 10's formats now; Q38 decides whether Phase 10's exit waits; §15 item 12a forbids claiming FR-3.4 in full otherwise | the channel media namespace, its read pairs and quota are open until the slice |
| R-19 | A single-proposal commit (a swap with its KeyPackage and evidence, or a context change naming 8 committers) exceeds the small class's 4 KiB floor at 32 members in a blank tree (the protocol review estimates 3.8–4.3 KB against 4 028) | medium / medium | V10-4 measures the composite worst cases; §5.7 splits multi-proposal commits; S0 lowers the small cap if needed and re-asks Q3 | a smaller small class |

## 17. Owner questions (the proposed default applies unless the owner objects)

| # | Question | Proposed default | Pe scurt (RO) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Approve ADR-40 … ADR-43 (Appendix A) as "propus", and this design as the basis for Phase 10 code? | **yes** | Aprobarea ADR-40 … ADR-43 și a designului |
| Q2 | Ciphersuite: classical 0x0003 for every P0 channel, with no PQ claim for channels and the §4.4 triggers; or the X-Wing suite 0x004D now (experimental code point, 16–64 KiB commits, cap ≈ 32 members, outside the audit, not migratable in place)? | **0x0003** | Canalele folosesc suita clasică; suita post-cuantică doar când e stabilă și revizuită |
| Q3 | Two size classes (small ≤ 32 members with 4 KiB blobs; large ≤ 100 with 16 KiB blobs), or only the small class in Phase 10 (saves about one working day, §14)? | **both** | Două clase: până la 32 de membri (4 KiB) și până la 100 (16 KiB) |
| Q4 | At most **9** channels per client (revised §19.8: 6 read pairs per channel while its old generation drains must fit the Phase 7 budget of 64 pairs, or a co-member who leaves every shared channel slows the DM inbox), beyond which the app refuses to join? **Declared (§19.23):** in the six weeks after a slot-table change the union relay sets can double the pairs, so the bound can be passed then (L8-C24); channel media with its own namespace would lower the limit to about 5 (§5.10, Q38). | **9** | Cel mult 9 canale pe dispozitiv (altfel un membru ostil poate încetini citirea DM-urilor) |
| Q5 | Invites as single-use bearer links or QR codes that carry the generation's namespace and access secrets, rather than invitations only through DMs? **Corrected (§19.7):** admission is automatic, so whoever presents a leaked unused invite first **becomes a member** and reads every later post until removed; every holder also sees the channel's traffic volume and can fill its quota until the next rotation (L8-C4). | **links and QR** (sendable inside a GHOST DM; QR in person recommended) | Invitația e un link sau QR de unică folosință; cine o folosește primul intră în canal — o invitație scursă înseamnă un membru nedorit |
| Q6 | A new namespace and new access secrets at every removal (3 tokens for the removing committer), so a removed member sees nothing more at the relays; or keep the namespace and declare that a removed member keeps seeing volume and timing? | **rotate** | La fiecare scoatere canalul trece pe o adresă nouă; cel scos nu mai vede nimic |
| Q7 | Funders: the owner by default, up to 3 named members; each pays 3 tokens per week per channel; non-funders pay nothing? **Declared (§19.9, LC-8):** under AD-1 the weekly redemptions single out the funders' clients, so the default makes the owner's client identifiable to the relays as the channel's funder; naming a non-owner funder moves that mark to the funder (the owner stays marked at the rotations it commits, Q31). | **yes** (the owner funds) | Proprietarul plătește releele canalului (3 token-uri pe săptămână); releele pot astfel recunoaște clientul care plătește |
| Q8 | Committers commit at scheduled passes only when work is pending (plus an empty weekly commit), declaring that relays can infer "a request was pending" (LC-6); or commit at every pass (more epochs, more storage writes, no LC-6)? | **when pending** | Moderatorul confirmă cererile la momente programate, nu imediat ce sosesc |
| Q9 | Members refresh their keys every 28 days; members idle 63 days are removed? | **yes** | Cheile membrilor se reîmprospătează la 28 de zile; cine lipsește 63 de zile e scos |
| Q10 | A 20-minute settle window before a membership change takes effect, anchored at the earlier of the member's own sighting and the relay's store hour (so a returning member catches up at once, §19.1)? | **20 min** | O schimbare de membri intră în vigoare după 20 de minute; cine revine după o absență se sincronizează imediat |
| Q11 | History policy "none" only (joiners see no content from before they joined; they do see earlier join requests of their generation, the rotation count and the epoch number, §8.4), no history transfer until a later ADR? | **yes** | Membrii noi nu văd conținutul de dinaintea intrării lor (văd doar cererile de intrare recente și numărul de rotații) |
| Q12 | Channels with timers ≤ 1 day: commits, join traffic and the administrative posts (`INVITE_ISSUED`, `LEAVE`, §19.21) live 7 days on relays (relays can tell them from posts there), or the whole channel at 7 days, or no timers below 7 days for channels? | **commits 7 days** | În canalele cu timer scurt, mesajele de administrare stau 7 zile pe relee |
| Q13 | One pseudonym per channel for life (a removed and re-added member returns with the same pseudonym; THREAT_MODEL:81 corrected)? | **yes** | Același pseudonim în același canal, pentru totdeauna |
| Q14 | Reveal to one member by encryption to its channel box key (fresh per leaf refresh, so a later theft of the recipient's seed opens no earlier reveal, §19.17); other members see that you revealed to someone, not to whom? (What the recipient can prove to others is Q35.) | **yes** | Dezvăluirea identității e vizibilă doar celui ales; ceilalți văd doar că ai făcut o dezvăluire |
| Q15 | Governance in Phase 10 limited to roles, removal, leave, eviction, sponsor records and the S5 threshold simulation; flags, quarantine, strikes, exclusion lists and verified channels with the Phase 13 UI? | **yes** | Rapoartele, carantina și strike-urile vin în Faza 13 |
| Q16 | Accept `hpke-rs` 0.7.0 (MPL-2.0, three crates) unmodified with published sources, or write a GHOST crypto provider on RustCrypto's `hpke`? | **accept** | Acceptăm `hpke-rs` (MPL-2.0) nemodificat, cu sursa publicată |
| Q17 | No external commits and no published GroupInfo (joins only through a committer)? | **yes** | Intrarea în canal doar prin aprobarea unui moderator |
| Q18 | Past epochs kept 2 days (1 day in short-timer channels, at most 64), deleted by count (§8.3); posts encrypted only when the channel is current; no native state cache in Phase 10? | **yes** | Cheile vechi se șterg după 2 zile; fără cache nativ |
| Q19 | Device loss: a restored device returns through a new invite and replaces its old leaf (a restore), **which rotates the channel's address so the lost device keeps no relay access** (+3 tokens for the committer, §19.16), whatever the member's rank; a running old device then shows "replaced"; a stolen seed can only be excluded by leaving the pseudonym and a new identity, and PCS does not heal against a seed thief (declared, L8-C9)? | **yes** | Un dispozitiv restaurat își reia locul printr-o invitație nouă, iar canalul își schimbă adresa ca dispozitivul pierdut să nu mai vadă nimic; un seed furat cere identitate nouă |
| Q20 | Owner actions: restore the CI artifact quota and provide a KVM runner (or accept the local emulator fallback) **before the S10 and S11 exits** (not before S0, §19.11); size the gate seeds from the measured nightly occupancy (V10-14); accept that nightly claims depend on the laptop's hours; run the device step on two arm64 phones before any distribution (§12.11)? | **yes** | Refacerea CI (cota de artefacte, runner cu KVM) înainte de porțile S10/S11, nu înainte de S0; două telefoane arm64 înainte de distribuție |
| Q21 | NFR-2 (p95 < 8 s) declared unmet by the 30-second listing interval, measured in Phase 12? | **yes** | Ținta de latență NFR-2 nu e atinsă cu programul actual; se măsoară în Faza 12 |
| Q22 | An mls-rs differential peer in tests? | **no** in Phase 10 (WG vectors suffice) | Fără a doua implementare MLS în teste |
| Q23 | A formal model (ProVerif) of the channel layer before distribution? | **not in Phase 10**; the external review decides | Fără model formal al canalelor acum |
| Q24 | No public channels, no discovery, no content readable by non-members in Phase 10; any of them needs its own ADR? | **yes** | Fără canale publice sau descoperire |
| Q25 | **Revised (§19.13):** moderators handle requests and evictions the owner has left pending for `rank × 48 h` (48 h for the first moderator, 96 h for the second, …), remove at their own action at any time, and make their own weekly PCS commit; only the owner changes the policy (and transfers rank 0); any committer may resign? (Round 10b's rule counted 48 h without an owner *commit*, which in a quiet channel made the first moderator active about five days in seven and raced the owner routinely.) | **yes** | Moderatorii preiau cererile lăsate de proprietar 48 h; pot scoate oricând; doar proprietarul schimbă regulile și își poate preda rolul |
| Q26 | One messaging actor for DMs and channels (one writer)? | **yes** | Un singur fir de lucru pentru DM și canale |
| Q27 | Fork policy (§19.1): a settled epoch is final and **sticky** (a later commit never moves a member); concurrent candidates are decided by successor senders, then rank and hash; a member leaves its branch only when it is dead (CO-11); live splits and relay partitions are flagged with a fingerprint for comparison out of band (CO-12). Or RFC 9750 §5.2.2's other option: merge at once and keep a copy of the previous group state for a few days so late commits can be judged — at the cost that a device seized in that window re-derives every later epoch, read posts included? | **sticky finality** | O epocă odată acceptată nu se mai schimbă; bifurcațiile se semnalează cu o amprentă de comparat în afara aplicației |
| Q28 | Rotate the channel's address and access secrets also when an invite of the generation expired unused, and when a generation is 28 days old (at most once per 14 days, never while an old address drains), so leaked or old invites stop giving metadata access and removals hide among routine rotations; cost +3 tokens per rotation for the committer (§19.7)? | **yes** | Canalul își schimbă adresa și după invitații expirate nefolosite și la 28 de zile, nu doar la scoateri |
| Q29 | Leave the channel name out of the invite link (the joiner sees it after admission; a leaked link does not name the channel), or carry it for a better accept screen (declared, L8-C4)? | **no name in the link** | Numele canalului nu apare în link-ul de invitație |
| Q30 | A sponsor-confirmed admission step (the sponsor sees the join request's key fingerprint and confirms it, compared with the invitee out of band, before a committer admits), which makes a leaked invite useless without the sponsor? | **not in P0**; flag bit 1 reserved for a per-channel choice in Phase 13 | Confirmarea de către sponsor a celui care intră: nu acum; bitul de politică rămâne rezervat |
| Q31 | Who funds a new generation after a rotation: the rotating committer at the switch hour of its commit (stores start at once; relays single out the committer, LC-8), or only the listed funders at their next renewal (stores wait up to a week; the committer stays unmarked unless it funds)? | **the rotating committer** | Cine plătește adresa nouă după o rotație: comitentul care a făcut-o (releele îl pot recunoaște) |
| Q32 | Split Welcomes into floor-sized fragments so they look like posts (hides the committer's Welcome store and the join count per blob), or keep one larger Welcome blob per joiner (declared LC-5)? | **not in P0**; Phase 11's blob fragmentation is the candidate | Welcome-urile rămân blob-uri mai mari (vizibile ca intrări) până în Faza 11 |
| Q33 | Rejoin after a fork or an expired log by a pseudonym-signed, fresh request admitted by a committer under the §6.7 rules (in-band, no new invite), or require every swap to be authorized by a new invite (restore only)? | **signed rejoin** | Reintrarea după o bifurcație se face cu o cerere semnată cu pseudonimul, fără invitație nouă |
| Q34 | The removal hold: while a commit that removes someone is settling (≤ 20 minutes), posts wait and are encrypted after it, so the removed member cannot read them; or post through the window (declared: the removed member reads those posts)? A committer's own non-removing commit holds nothing (§19.13). | **hold** | Cât se aplică o scoatere (≤ 20 de minute), postările așteaptă, ca cel scos să nu le poată citi |
| Q35 | **Reveal proof (§19.17).** The reveal carries `sig_id` (the `ghost1` identity key signs the pseudonym binding) and `sig_ps`: the recipient can prove to anyone — an operator, a court, other members — that this pseudonym is that identity. Keep it and declare it (L8-C8; Phase 13 warns before a reveal), or a deniable reveal: no identity signature in the channel, the binding confirmed afterwards inside the recipient's first DM with the card (Olm messages carry no signatures, so the recipient can show nothing a third party must believe), at the cost of a second step and a new design in Phase 13? Either way the MLS signature on the REVEAL post proves which pseudonym sent that ciphertext. | **keep, declared**; the deniable variant designed with Phase 13's reveal UX | Cel căruia îi dezvălui identitatea poate dovedi oricui legătura (semnătură): declarat acum; varianta fără dovadă transferabilă, în Faza 13 |
| Q36 | **Seizure window in short-timer channels (§19.19).** A seized device opens every unconsumed message of its current epoch from the epoch's start (up to 7 days with the weekly PCS commit, for anyone who recorded the relays). In channels with timers ≤ 1 d, make the committers' PCS commit daily (about one epoch a day, one 20-minute settle, no tokens), so the window stays near the timer, as ADR-27 promises? | **yes** (daily in channels with timers ≤ 1 d; weekly otherwise) | În canalele cu timer scurt, cheile se reîmprospătează zilnic, ca un telefon confiscat să nu deschidă mesaje mai vechi de o zi |
| Q37 | **Owner absence (§19.13).** An owner who vanishes without transferring rank 0 freezes the policy for the channel's life and keeps a leaf nobody can evict (a PCS hole if its device state leaked). Accept and declare it, or add a succession rule (rank 1 takes rank 0 after the owner's silence)? Without a server there is no trusted time: an epoch-count rule can be pumped by a moderator's empty commits, and a commit minute is the committer's claim, so any rule lets a moderator take over an active but quiet owner. | **accept, declared** (L8-C16); the UI urges naming a moderator and transferring before leaving | Dacă proprietarul dispare fără să-și predea rolul, regulile canalului rămân înghețate; fără succesiune automată (ar permite unui moderator să preia canalul) |
| Q38 | **Channel media (C-17, §5.10).** Spec FR-3.4 requires media up to 50 MiB in posts; Phase 11's design leaves channel media to Phase 10 ("designed and gated with Phase 10", its Q17), and channel media needs Phase 11's engine. Does Phase 10's exit wait for a channel-media slice built after Phase 11's engine lands, or does Phase 10 close on its plan row with the contract reserved and FR-3.4's media half recorded as not met until that slice (owned jointly, gated then in `channel-exit-gate`)? The slice also decides the media namespace (a separate one per generation costs about 4 of the 9 channels, §5.10). | **close Phase 10 without it**; the channel-media slice runs after Phase 11's engine, FR-3.4 stays partly open until then, and Phase 11's Q17 text is aligned at merge | Media în canale: formatul e rezervat acum; implementarea după motorul de media al Fazei 11; Faza 10 se închide fără ea |
| Q39 | **Order of decisions (FEA-M6, §19.23).** This design builds on ADR-25 and ADR-30′ … ADR-39 (proposed, awaiting the owner as Phase 9's Q4) and on Phase 9's code, whose exit gate is open. May S0 start now (evidence only), S1 onwards only once those ADRs are decided or explicitly accepted as the basis, and may Phase 10 close before Phase 9 with every claim marked as inheriting Phase 9's open gate? A green T2 sweep is not a Phase 10 exit item (§7.9). | **yes** (S0 now; S1 after the ADR decision; closure before Phase 9 allowed, marked) | Faza 10 poate începe cu S0 acum; restul după decizia pe ADR-urile Fazei 9; se poate închide înaintea Fazei 9, cu mențiune |

## 18. Findings outside Phase 10 (proposed as separate tasks)

- **F-1.** STATUS:14 and `relay/README.md:20` assign "Noise gossip" to Phase 10; that is spec v2.0 numbering (its Phase 10 was multi-relay metadata protection). The v2.1 plan places relay-to-relay work nowhere explicitly; the owner should assign it (Phase 12 or operations). Erratum in Appendix B.4.
- **F-2.** The guarantee matrix (plan:241), TM:50 (AD-2 "nu vede … apartenență") and TM:38 (AS-7 "doar în capetele MLS") say relays do not see channel membership; under AD-1 session co-occurrence rebuilds it (L7-D2). Text corrected in Appendix B; decoupling is Phase 12.
- **F-3.** Spec FR-5.5 (90-day default TTL) is superseded for channel and DM blobs by ADR-27/ADR-32; the spec erratum should say so.
- **F-4.** Phase 9's G-R3, G-R4 (graph half), G-R7 and G-R8 are not built; building them once, generic over the DM and MLS graphs, is cheaper than twice (S0).
- **F-5.** Upstream OpenMLS requests (no fork): a storage transaction hook (begin/commit around an operation), which would let GHOST drop the change-set copy; a sender-data peek for ordering; a documented time source for past-epoch stamps; **an injectable time source for leaf and KeyPackage lifetime validation** (today `SystemTime::now()`, switchable only by a crate-internal test flag, §19.4), which would let GHOST judge lifetimes on its trusted clock and let the JVM worlds run on virtual time.
- **F-6.** The v1 `media` table has `owner_operation_id` without a foreign key (`Schema.kt:211-219`); Phase 11 should replace it with the migration-7 style. (The Phase 11 design's migration M drops `media` and `media_blobs` behind a fail-closed guard, `faza11-media.md` D21; migration 7 of this design leaves them to it, so the two agree.)
- **F-9.** The plan's FR-3.4 row reads "Timestamp rotunjit la minut" (plan:262), while spec FR-3.4 reads "Posts MUST support text, media up to 50 MiB and an authenticated client timestamp", accepted by "Boundary-size, invalid-media and timestamp-signature tests pass" (spec p.8). The plan row should carry the media clause; which phase closes it is Q38 (C-17).
- **F-7.** Phase 9's harness debts that Phase 10 must build (§19.10): the seeded native randomness (never built; F9 §22 item 13), the redeem lane inside the world, HIGH mode with send delays, circuit timing, and the fuzz and vendor-test arrangements. Built once, generic over the DM and MLS worlds, they also close parts of Phase 9's own open gate; STATUS should say so when they land.
- **F-8.** THREAT_MODEL S3 ("MLS transcript detects gaps") overclaims against a delivery service that partitions the group: RFC 9750 §5.2 says such a partition "can be detected only by out-of-band comparison". The wording is corrected in Appendix B.2.

---

## 19. Normative corrections after review (round 10b)

**The review.** Round 10a (commit `a79b7ce`) was reviewed adversarially under three lenses: protocol and cryptographic soundness (findings `P10-Rn`), privacy, metadata and threat coverage (`PRV-Hn`), and feasibility, testability, scope and consistency (`FEA-Hn`, `FEA-Mn`). Every finding received was checked against the OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources in the local cargo registry (`om:`; the crate's `.cargo_vcs_info.json` names commit `3a3e35de3feeca8f6605143c464d5452ae584d43`, which is the tag `openmls-v0.9.0`), against RFC 9750 (text fetched 2026-09-25 and quoted below) and against the repository at `f244b80` (`InboxStore.kt`, `Inbox.kt`, `Schema.kt`, `PairSchedule.kt`, `TrafficPolicy.kt`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`, `docs/ci-self-hosted.md`, F7, F9). **No finding received was rejected as wrong** (round 10c rejects no finding in full; it rejects parts of two — P10-R8, P10-R13 — narrows two — P10-R9, PRV-M2 — and declines two optional suggestions — P10-R5, PRV-M5 — each with its reason in §19.0a). Eight proposed fixes are narrowed, replaced or left to the owner, each with its reason in its subsection: P10-R1's "late commit loses at the source" and its optional pre-merge copy (§19.1, Q27); P10-R3's ±1-day window and "no fork evidence" test (§19.3); FEA-H3's "authenticated commit minute" anchor (§19.1); PRV-H3's sponsor-confirmed admission (§19.7, Q30) and its rotation after every expired invite (spaced to one per 14 days, §19.7); PRV-H5's switch at "the first session after the commit's day plus 1" (replaced by the commit's switch hour, which is commit-determined without a day-long pause in posting, §19.8); PRV-H6's reconsidered funder default (kept, with the leak declared and the alternatives offered, §19.9, Q7, Q31).

**What each round read (revised in round 10c).** A tooling defect cut each review at 10 000 characters before round 10b's author saw it: the protocol lens inside P10-R4's correction (P10-R5's title and everything after it unread; P10-R3 referred to a P10-R15 whose text was not delivered), the privacy lens inside PRV-H7's fix (PRV-M1 … PRV-M6 and PRV-L1 … PRV-L4 unread), the feasibility lens inside FEA-M1 (its second half, FEA-M2 … FEA-M8, six low findings, a table of eight proposed owner questions and a table of claims "verified but not true" unread). Round 10b corrected P10-R5 from a reconstruction (§19.5), P10-R15 from P10-R3's reference (§19.3) and FEA-M1 from its first half (§19.6). **Round 10c read the three reviews in full** — `p10-review-protocol.md` (22 935 characters), `p10-review-privacy.md` (15 808), `p10-review-feasibility.md` (16 947), kept under `.claude/scratch/reviews/` outside version control — and checked **every** finding, before and after the cut, against the round-10b text, the repository at `d0fddf8`, the OpenMLS 0.9.0 sources and, where a finding touched it, the Phase 11 design on branch `p11-design` at `116cfcd`. The result is §19.0a: 55 findings (17 protocol, 17 privacy, 21 feasibility) and the feasibility lens's 8 proposed questions; **none remains unread or unanswered**. Round 10b's reconstruction of P10-R5 matched the reviewer's text except for one optional suggestion, which §19.13 declines with its reason. The corrections of round 10c are §19.13 … §19.24; §19.25 is their bookkeeping.

**This section is normative: where it and an earlier section disagree, this section wins.** Earlier sections were corrected in place where the change is local; those places cite "(§19.n)".
